#2854. Federal Reserve Board announces pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, and changes to streamline and adjust their check services

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 1:30 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, such as the clearing of checks, automated clearing house (ACH) transactions, instant payments, and wholesale payment and settlement services.

By law, the Federal Reserve must establish fees to recover the costs, including imputed costs, of providing payment services over the long run. The Federal Reserve expects to recover 101.5 percent of actual and imputed expenses in 2027, including the return on equity that would have been earned if a private-sector firm provided the services. Overall, price changes for 2027 will result in an estimated 1.4 percent average price increase for established, mature services.

In addition, the Federal Reserve Banks announced changes to streamline and adjust their check services. As described in the request for information on check services issued last year, the volume of checks processed by the Federal Reserve Banks is declining, but checks remain an important payment instrument for many consumers and businesses. The changes were informed by public feedback and ensure that the Federal Reserve Banks continue to provide check services efficiently. The adjustments include optimizing check processing windows during low-volume periods, increasing fees that banks and credit unions pay to process checks, and other operational updates. These changes do not impact consumers' and businesses' ability to pay with or receive checks and only impact how banks and credit unions process checks with Federal Reserve Banks.

The entire 2027 fee schedule for all of the Federal Reserve's payment services is published on FRBservices.org®. Additional information on the Federal Reserve's payments services, including service usage statistics, is available on the Board's website.

#2851. Agencies extend comment period for proposed third-party risk management guidance

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation, the Federal Reserve Board, the National Credit Union Administration, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that they will extend, until December 16, the comment period for proposed guidance on managing risks associated with third-party relationships. Comments on the proposal were originally due November 16.

The proposed guidance reflects the agencies' supervisory experience and lessons learned from examining financial institutions' third-party risk management practices. It is intended to help banks and credit unions tailor their risk management practices to the risks associated with individual third-party relationships. The proposed guidance takes a principles-based approach and, like all supervisory guidance, is non-binding.

When finalized, the federal bank regulatory agencies plan to rescind existing third-party risk management guidance and replace it with the finalized guidance to promote consistency and prudent innovation in the banking industry. NCUA also plans to rescind its existing third-party risk management guidance and will conduct its own reviews to update related guidance as needed.

Also today, the Federal Reserve Board separately announced that it will extend, until December 16, the comment period on a proposed third-party risk management guide specifically for Federal Reserve-supervised traditional community banks.

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guidance; Extension of Comment Period (PDF)

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guide for Traditional Community Banking Organizations (PDF)

Agencies seek comment on proposed third-party risk management guidance and issue statement on community bank engagement with core service providers

#2845. Wan’Dale Robinson injury could cost Giants in 2027 NFL Draft

The Tennessee Titans are putting wide receiver Wan'Dale Robinson on injured reserve with an ankle injury, and that could be costly for the New York Giants.

According to the compensatory pick formula, Robinson is the only former Giant the team is expected to receive a compensatory pick for in the 2027 NFL Draft.

Compensatory formula expert Nick Korte of Over the Cap believes that Robinson missing time could drop the compensatory pick the Giants receive for losing Robinson in free agency from the fourth to the fifth round.

Robinson has caught just 38 passes for 272 yards for the Titans. He is averaging just 4.2 catches per game after averaging 5.5 and 5.8 per game the last two seasons with the Giants. Robinson's 7.2 yards per catch is also a career low.

#2843. 'Who and what comes next': Analysts expect Nvidia investor uncertainty despite earnings beat

Analysts expect uncertainty around Nvidia's future to linger despite another earnings beat, noting that much of the third quarter was already locked in before the turmoil that peaked in Jensen Huang's resignation as CEO.

Nvidia's third-quarter revenue came in at $115.84 billion, more than $6 billion above analysts' expectations of $109.10 billion, while fourth-quarter guidance of $130 billion topped the $125.1 billion consensus, according to LSEG.

Part of that increase reflects an extra week in the fiscal fourth quarter, which Melius Research estimated accounts for $9 billion to $10 billion of the sequential jump.

The company also lifted its fiscal 2028 growth forecast to 75% from 70%, even with China excluded from its data center compute revenue outlook.

"That's a team going full steam ahead, rather than just steadying the ship, and a clear signal that management believes next year can run just fine without Jensen Huang at the helm," said Josh Gilbert, eToro's lead analyst for Asia Pacific and the Middle East.

Still, even with the results that he called "nothing short of astonishing", Gilbert said "investors will still be left with the uncertainty of who and what comes next."

In his first statement since he resigned last week, Huang said before the results release that he was open to returning to the company. However, chair of Nvidia's board Harvey Jones said on the earnings call that the board does not intend to reinstate him as CEO.

The results should help ease some concerns created by Nvidia's recent leadership turmoil, said Morningstar senior equity analyst Brian Colello, who described the figures as "solid" and broadly in line with the firm's expectations.

Colello expressed continued confidence that "the AI infrastructure buildout is real," pointing to Nvidia's shipments holding steady in the third quarter.

Michael Wilson, chief investment officer and chief U.S. equity strategist at Morgan Stanley, told CNBC TV that the results were "somewhat expected," with no major drop-off in Nvidia's business anticipated in the near term and guidance remaining "pretty constructive."

Wilson said AI remained a "desirable investment," with no slowdown in spending seen or expected from hyperscalers and some neoclouds — newer cloud providers built around AI computing. However, he added that investment may not continue at the pace investors expected six months ago.

Nvidia is widely seen as the bellwether for the broader AI industry and spending.

Gilbert shared that view, saying the AI trade can now "let out a small sigh of relief" after holding its breath for weeks. Nvidia had been "firing on all cylinders" before Huang's departure, he said, and its underlying fundamentals remained intact.

He pointed to Nvidia's supply commitments, which rose to $390 billion from $279 billion in three months, as well as its Vera Rubin platform, which has already brought in $20 billion.

"It's the clearest sign yet that the AI boom still has plenty of fuel in the tank," Gilbert said.

Nvidia shares rose more than 5% in after-hours trading, though Gilbert said any rally is expected to meet resistance and "plenty of volatility" in the coming weeks, with the CEO search only just getting under way and investigations still playing out.

New U.S. export restrictions announced this week add another wrinkle. The Commerce Department will require licenses for shipments of advanced chips to Singapore, Malaysia, Indonesia, Thailand and Brazil, citing the risk that they could be diverted to China.

Gilbert said the direct impact on Nvidia's forecasts appears limited because its guidance already assumed no China data center revenue, while China accounted for only around 2% of sales this quarter. However, new licensing requirements for shipments to places such as Singapore and Malaysia could slow some neocloud build-outs, he said.

Melius Research analyst Ben Reitzes said fiscal 2028 is when sales to countries covered by the new restrictions would be expected to decline. He interpreted Nvidia's forecast as a sign that hyperscalers will take whatever supply becomes available elsewhere.

Nvidia said it is still studying the new Commerce Department rules but remains confident in its 75% growth target.

Colello pointed to longer-term risks around policy, international sales and hyperscalers developing their own chips, though he said Nvidia should remain an important player as the market expands.

He said investors will now be looking for the company to reaffirm its long-term outlook and show it can continue executing through the leadership transition.

"We're at a point of peak uncertainty because investors still don't know what Nvidia's long-term leadership will look like," Colello added.

"Jensen Huang's departure could create some near-term disruption, but a credible succession plan should go a long way toward steadying the ship. The next CEO will have big shoes to fill, but Nvidia's long-term opportunity does not hinge on one person."

#2841. LeBron Log No. 5: Popcorn in hand, a viral chorus and ‘basketball AI'

LeBron Log No. 5: Popcorn in hand, a viral chorus and 'basketball AI' originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

LeBron James spent Monday night in street clothes.

He still managed to argue a call with popcorn in his hand, practice his golf swing during a timeout and reject a report about himself before the Sixers left Brooklyn with a win.

That followed a Sunday afternoon in which James passed Jason Kidd on the NBA's career assists list and received an unusual description from Pacers head coach Rick Carlisle.

Welcome to the fifth edition of the LeBron Log.

James sat out the Sixers' 135-123 win over the Nets because of low back and sciatica management.

It was his fourth absence through 15 games. James has yet to play both ends of a back-to-back this season after logging nearly 33 minutes Sunday against Indiana.

He watched Monday's game from the end of the Sixers' bench in a yellow hat and an "Ohio Baby" sweatshirt. James snacked from a small bag before switching to popcorn.

When Joel Embiid believed he was fouled on a first-half drive, James stood and pleaded his case to official Jacyn Goble without putting down the box. He also joined conversations between teammates and officials during several timeouts.

James found another way to pass a third-quarter stoppage. As "Hot To Go" played inside Barclays Center, he practiced his golf swing near the bench.

His most pointed contribution came on Instagram.

An ESPN story by Dave McMenamin cited a Sixers source who said James had asked the coaching staff to remain patient while he worked toward finding his rhythm and conditioning by December.

James reposted ESPN's graphic and rejected the account.

"🧢! Those words never came out of my mouth!" he wrote.

The Sixers improved to 4-0 without James. They have beaten the Bucks, Hornets, Raptors and Nets in the games he has missed.

Brooklyn pulled within five points during the final five minutes Monday, putting the Sixers in another clutch game.

Philadelphia has reached 10 clutch wins more quickly than almost any team since the statistic began being tracked in 1996-97. The 2026-27 Sixers are the second team in that span with at least 10 such victories through their first 15 games.

The other was the 1997-98 Atlanta Hawks, who had 12.

James was also on the last team before Philadelphia to open a season with at least 10 consecutive clutch wins. The Lakers started 13-0 in those games last season.

James did not play in the Sixers' latest close finish, but he has now been part of the last two teams to start at least 10-0 under those circumstances.

Before Sunday's game, Carlisle explained why he tries to limit his play calls against James.

"LeBron James is one of the reasons we try not to have play calls, 'cause he knows all of them," Carlisle said. "He's a walking NBA basketball AI machine. That's what he is."

James followed with 15 points, 11 assists and seven rebounds in the Sixers' 131-124 win.

His pass to VJ Edgecombe for a layup with 1:19 remaining gave him 12,092 career assists and moved him past Kidd for third in NBA history. Only Chris Paul and John Stockton remain ahead of him.

Noah Levick wrote more about James' milestone and the anticipation behind his passes.

When told what Carlisle had called him, James appreciated the description.

"So, yeah, Rick is great," James said. "He's one of the greatest coaches of all time, and I've had my battles with him over the years. That's super humbling coming from a guy like that."

Philadelphia has found its soundtrack for James' biggest home moments.

The Sixers have played the viral "LeBron, LeBron, LeBron" song after several of his baskets, including his late putback against the Knicks on Oct. 30.

The refrain comes from a version of Steve Lacy's "Oh Yeah?" that replaces "Come on" with James' name. The Baylor University Men's Choir performed its own rendition, helping the song spread across social media.

Lacy later joined in. During an Oct. 15 concert at Freedom Mortgage Pavilion in Camden, he performed the LeBron version as the crowd sang along.

James has noticed Philadelphia's use of it.

"I've seen it. I've heard it. It's pretty hilarious, to be honest," James said. "I heard it towards the end of a game when we were winning. I made a play, and I heard that song. I was just chuckling to myself on the inside."

#2836. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on the growth potential of these 3 stocks

Fears around the artificial intelligence trade and higher pressure on oil prices are weighing on global markets, but a few stocks offer investors solid growth prospects even in choppy times.

Investors with a long-term horizon can look beyond the ongoing volatility and track the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts to pick attractive stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Health insurer UnitedHealth delivered better-than-expected results for the third quarter of 2026. The company also raised its full-year earnings outlook, reflecting continued progress on its turnaround plan.

Following the Q3 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Erin Wright reiterated a buy rating on UNH stock with a price target of $529. "UNH's beat & raise reinforces turnaround momentum, with improving MA [Medicare Advantage]/Optum Health supporting a higher '26 EPS baseline & upside to '27 consensus," said Wright.

The five-star analyst added that better-than-expected performance in the UnitedHealthcare and Optum Health businesses supported another upgrade to the company's 2026 adjusted earnings per share outlook to at least $20.50. Wright noted that UNH raised the earnings guidance despite incremental investments planned for the fourth quarter of 2026.

Furthermore, Wright highlighted management's view that the consensus 2027 EPS of $22.63 was too conservative. Reaching even the low end of the company's 13% to 16% long-term EPS growth target would imply an EPS of $23.17.

Wright raised her 2026 EPS estimate to $20.65 from $19.82 and 2027 EPS to $23.24 from $22.54, reflecting the latest upside and an improving 2026 medical loss ratio to 87.4% from 88.1%. Overall, she believes that UNH's operational performance continues to improve, increasing her confidence in its UHC strategy and Optum Health turnaround execution.

Wright ranks No. 264 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 15%. See UnitedHealth Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Social media giant Meta Platforms has been making headlines since the launch of its Muse AI agent, which is seeing solid traction. Investors look forward to updates on the company's AI strategy when it announces its third-quarter results on Oct. 21.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Guggenheim analyst Michael Morris maintained a buy rating on Meta stock and increased his price target to $850 from $700, reflecting higher estimates for 2027. He expects Muse usage momentum and engagement levels to shape investor sentiment into the Q3 earnings call.

Specifically, Morris highlighted that, according to Apptopia, Muse reached 1.3 million daily active users in five weeks versus 300,000 in the first week. Given that Muse's current adoption represents less than 0.05% of Meta's 3.6 billion daily active user base, the analyst expects management to prioritize boosting usage over near-term monetization.

"Over time, we believe Muse monetizes primarily through businesses paying to reach consumers through their agents, consistent with the economics of Meta's core advertising business, rather than consumer wallets," said the five-star analyst. He added that the checkout integrations with Shopify and PayPal reflect this framework.

While there are concerns that a rival could overtake Muse or the recently launched Muse Wonder and push Meta into a prolonged cycle of overinvestment, Morris remains bullish due to the company's strong record in scaling WhatsApp and competing strongly with TikTok via Reels as well as free distribution to an extensive user base.

Morris ranks No. 629 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Meta Platforms KPIs on TipRanks.

Next is semiconductor company Marvell Technology. Earlier this month, Marvell held its Investor Day, where it raised its total addressable market estimate to $400 billion by 2030, reflecting solid AI-led demand tailwinds.

After hosting a non-deal roadshow with Marvell's senior management following the Investor Day, Stifel analyst Tore Svanberg reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $370.

The five-star analyst said that a major focus of the roadshow was the XPU and XPU Attach mix, as Marvell's Custom business expands toward its FY31 revenue target of $30 billion. In this regard, management reiterated that it expects a balanced mix of XPU and XPU Attach revenue in FY31, though it noted that either segment could grow rapidly enough to shift the mix toward 60/40 or 40/60.

While XPU drove most of the nearly $1.5 billion Custom revenue in FY26, Svanberg expects Marvell's XPU Attach business to grow substantially through the end of the decade, leading to a more balanced mix in this business. At the Investor Day event, the company raised its FY29 Custom target from more than $10 billion to over $12 billion, mainly driven by strength in the XPU Attach business.

Svanberg said that another key topic in the roadshow was whether Marvell can secure enough capacity to meet its raised targets. Management said that it met with suppliers ahead of Investor Day to share its five-year goals and received commitments to support these targets. The analyst believes that Marvell's supply chain commitments give it a competitive edge over rivals as customers seek reliable partners for large-scale infrastructure buildouts.

Svanberg ranks No. 91 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 31.8%. See Marvell Technology Statistics on TipRanks.

#2834. Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double

Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers stayed unbeaten Saturday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena, earning a 127-116 win over the Bucks and improving to 3-0 on the season.

Joel Embiid had his 10th career regular-season triple-double, tallying 28 points, 11 rebounds and 10 assists.

On his 30th birthday, Jaylen Brown posted 27 points and five assists. Tyrese Maxey had 26 points. VJ Edgecombe scored 17 and grabbed 13 boards.

The Sixers' absences on the first night of a back-to-back included LeBron James (low back/sciatica management). Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said pregame that he did not know yet who would be available for his team's matchup with the Pistons on Sunday.

Here are observations on the Sixers' victory Saturday:

Dean Wade made his Sixers debut and started in James' place. He'd missed the first two games of the regular season with a left ankle sprain.

The 3-and-D forward scored the night's first points by a sinking a corner three-pointer on the opening possession.

Embiid also had a sharp start. He moved well, made good decisions and served as the main hub of his team's offense in the early going.

The Sixers' seven-time All-Star center has often enjoyed facing Myles Turner. He entered the night with an average of 29.4 points in 18 previous games against the Bucks big man.

Embiid did a little bit of everything in the first quarter, posting eight points, four assists and four rebounds over nine minutes. After snatching an offensive board, Embiid drained a fadeaway jumper to give the Sixers a 10-1 lead.

One of Embiid's assists was quite stylish. He found Edgecombe open beyond the arc with a behind-the-back bounce pass. Edgecombe knocked down the shot and wound up going 3 for 4 from three-point range in the first quarter.

The Sixers added rookie Labaron Philon Jr. to their rotation. Jabari Walker stayed in the mix, though he didn't enter until the 7:36 mark of the second quarter.

Philon played four minutes in his NBA debut and missed his lone shot attempt, a corner three. On defense, Philon allowed a couple of blow-bys off the dribble. He generally looked like he belonged on offense, albeit without any production in his brief run.

Maxey banked in a half-court heave at the first-quarter buzzer to put the Sixers up nine points. The Sixers couldn't carry forward any momentum, however, and began the second quarter poorly.

Adem Bona had another rough stint amid a subpar start to the season. After Anfernee Simons missed a transition layup try, Ryan Rollins' three lifted the Bucks into a 40-37 lead.

The dreadful stretch for the Sixers on both sides of the ball called to mind how they'd fallen into a 29-point deficit Thursday night vs. the Cavaliers before clawing out a comeback win.

The Sixers mustered just seven points in first six minutes of the second quarter and had many stagnant possessions that concluded with contested jumpers. The team's transition defense was dismal for much of the second period. Milwaukee had the game's first 19 fast-break points.

The Sixers regained the lead late in the second quarter. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope made two three-balls in the second and nabbed three first-half steals. Embiid assisted a go-ahead, and-one Walker layup.

Brown hit a three with 11.8 seconds left in the second quarter. Unsurprisingly, his variety of shotmaking skills were useful without James in the lineup.

Even when James or Embiid sit, the Sixers should have a simple advantage in terms of volume of stars over most opponents this year. None of Brown, Embiid or Maxey had the sort of off night that would have improved the Bucks' odds of a road win. They'd all passed the 20-point mark by the end of the third quarter.

Maxey notched another final-possession bucket, this time with a more conventional make. He canned a jumper from the right elbow with 1.7 seconds left in the third to extend the Sixers' lead to 97-87.

The Bucks made a late push in the fourth quarter. Kel'el Ware's put-back, and-one layup cut the Milwaukee's deficit to 117-114.

The Sixers ultimately handled business. Embiid reached his triple-double by assisting a second-chance Brown jumper and and Caldwell-Pope soon drilled a dagger corner three. Embiid even threw down a last-minute dunk for good measure.

For the second consecutive season, the Sixers have a 3-0 start. They got to four wins last year before dropping their first game.

#2832. JPMorgan is working on AI agent transactions that don’t involve humans

Artificial intelligence agents have already proven to be helpful, or cause a lot of trouble, or both. One area seen as high-potential is in payments, where — if they're able to be trusted — the agents could deal with all sorts of money matters on behalf of people.

That's why JPMorgan Chase and South Korea's Hyundai Card are testing how AI agents can handle financial transactions without direct human intervention, including whether they can eventually negotiate and make payments with one another.

The companies have completed an initial proof-of-concept involving a simulated foreign exchange transaction and are targeting the first half of 2027 for a more complex phase involving multiple AI agents.

Wee Kee Toh, global head of Kinexys Insights at Kinexys by JP Morgan, told CNBC that the next step is to test what happens when autonomous agents move beyond execution and begin interacting with each other in an ecosystem.

"That, I would say, is really the biggest part of what we want to get as an expansion of the phase one scope," he added.

The initial trial involved a Hyundai Card AI agent requesting a foreign exchange transaction to maintain euro liquidity in its Kinexys blockchain deposit account. JPMorgan said it was the first private blockchain-based autonomous bank-agent-to-client-agent test for corporate treasury management.

While the initial trial has been completed, JPMorgan has yet to set a timeline for commercializing the technology.

Toh said building the technology itself was relatively straightforward, but establishing appropriate governance and internal controls proved more difficult.

"Technology-wise, it's really not the biggest hurdle," he said, pointing instead to questions around accountability and the risks of allowing AI agents to execute financial transactions without direct human oversight.

However, the potential of such agents to change interactions with financial services has to come with equivalent accountability, according to experts.

Data management and protection software company AvePoint found in its State of AI 2026 report that unauthorized autonomous agent actions were the third most common AI agent-related security breach, affecting 34.1% of companies that reported such incidents.

Those actions included executing transaction and modifying data without approval, though the findings were not isolated to corporate treasury payments.

"When agents negotiate and execute autonomously … governance shifts from monitoring human behavior to monitoring machine-to-machine dependencies," said AvePoint CEO Tianyi Jiang.

Jiang explained that interacting AI agents could amplify errors and trigger transactions before humans can intervene.

“The worst-case scenario is a cascading failure where one compromised or hallucinating agent feeds bad data to another,” he said, potentially “triggering a high-speed chain reaction of flawed financial transfers before a human even realizes it is happening.”

To address such concerns, Kinexys developed a framework that restricts AI agents to predefined transaction parameters, automatically rejecting requests that fall outside authorized limits.

"You are going to draw a box around the agent. The agent will never be able to do anything out of the box," Toh said.

For example, an AI agent authorized to transact up to $5 million would be blocked from executing transactions above that threshold, he said.

Hassan Ahmed, Coinbase's Singapore country director, said safe agentic payments will require clear guardrails, including spending limits, restricted account permissions and explicit authorization requirements.

Users should also be able to monitor activity and revoke access at any time, he said, given the potential for AI to create new opportunities for malicious actors.

"While private or permissioned environments may offer additional controls for certain use cases, we should not lose sight of the potential of open blockchain networks to support more accessible, interoperable and efficient financial systems," Ahmed said.

JPMorgan and Hyundai Card are also exploring whether to extend their collaboration to other businesses within Hyundai Motor Group, whose broader operations were one reason the bank selected the credit card company as a partner, Toh said. No specific projects have been announced.

Hyundai Motor Group holds a combined 78.04% stake in Hyundai Card through Hyundai Motor, Kia and Hyundai Commercial, according to the card issuer's website.

Hyundai Card has previously experimented with blockchain-based cross-border payments. In July, the company said it completed a proof-of-concept involving a $20,000 transfer using the USDT stablecoin between Hyundai Motor's U.S. and Mexican subsidiaries, with the transfer and verification process taking an average of seven minutes.

Kinexys has also been expanding its blockchain-based payments business. The JPMorgan unit said in August that it had processed more than $4 trillion in transactions since inception, averaging about $7 billion a day.

Separately, JPMorgan launched a proof-of-concept for its dollar-denominated deposit token, JPMD, in June 2025, before making it available to institutional clients that November.

#2831. Highs and lows of Fernando Mendoza’s starting debut for Las Vegas Raiders

Fernando Mendoza's first NFL start in the Las Vegas Raiders' Week 10 matchup against the Seattle Seahawks was a tale of two halves.

In the first half, he completed 11 of 13 passes for 90 yards, with both incompletions being throwaways, and had five rushes for 21 yards and a touchdown. According to Sumer Sports, that resulted in a total EPA of 6.56, an EPA per play of 0.328 and a 55 percent success rate — pretty good numbers. However, the second half was a different story, going six-for-16 with 53 passing yards and one rush for one yard to post a negative 13.49 total EPA, negative 0.642 EPA per play and a 14.3 percent success rate.

For the entire game, the No. 1 pick completed 17 of 29 passes (58.6 percent) for 143 passing yards, along with six rushes for 22 yards and a touchdown. That netted a negative 6.93 total EPA, negative 0.169 EPA per play and a 34.1 percent success rate.

For more context, Pro Football Focus credited him with a 66.7 percent adjusted completion percentage (accounting for the two throwaways and one drop), a slightly below-baseline (60.0) 59.8 passing grade and a slightly above-baseline rushing grade (65.2) to sit at a 62.2 overall mark.

While the numbers above help paint part of the picture, let's dive into the tape to see the full story.

To help get the rookie going, Klint Kubiak dialed up bootlegs on Mendoza's first three throws. The clip above is the third one and is a great decision by the quarterback.

His first read is to see if the go route from the outside receiver is available for a one-on-one opportunity. However, the Seahawks have that covered with the corner underneath and the boundary safety on top of the receiver to execute the Cover 2 side of the Cover 6 call.

Mendoza's next two options are either tight end Michael Mayer, who runs a chip release into a curl route, or wide receiver Jalen Nailor on a crossing/drag route. The MIKE linebacker is all over Mayer to take that away, and the WILL linebacker turns to ROBOT and help the corner against Nailor. Meanwhile, fullback Connor Heyward is working across the formation to sell the run fake and run a flat route.

Heyward gets contacted by the edge rusher, who ends up getting pressure on Mendoza. However, Mendoza does a good job of going through his reads, finding Heyward and throwing around the free rusher to dump it to the fullback, who picks up five on second and six to set up a third-and-short situation.

This isn't a sexy throw by any means, but it's a good process to put the offense in a favorable third down situation.

Here, we'll get a look at the touchdown run that got Allegiant Stadium rocking and is eerily similar to the run the Indiana product had to win the National Championship over Miami last January.

It's third and 10 as the Raiders come out in an empty formation where the Seahawks respond with a five-man box and both safeties deep pre-snap. That's the perfect look to run a quarterback draw into, and Mendoza sells it well by taking a drop with his first step and eyeing wide receivers Jalen Nailor and Tre Tucker post-snap, keeping the safeties in the end zone.

Then, he takes off and does a great job of setting up left guard Spencer Burford's block by getting downfield and pressing the five-yard line. That allows Burford to position himself between the linebacker and ball carrier. Finally, Mendoza shows off some impressive vision to see the field safety overpursue, cut back to the other hashmark and put the ball in the painted area.

If the previous clip was the No. 1 pick's best play of the game, the clip above is the best throw he made on the afternoon.

Seattle starts with a two-high look pre-snap before rotating the boundary safety into the hook-to-curl area to play Cover 3. That takes away the curl from Nailor on the curl-flat concept. Maybe Mendoza can hit Mayer on the flat route, but the linebacker manages to avoid the pick and stay on top of the route. So, that's a fine decision to hold onto the ball. But the problem is that the pocket starts to break down with Trey Zuhn III getting bull-rushed by Byron Murphy II.

Mendoza manages to get out of trouble, but he can settle down and pick his eyes up at the 10-yard line instead of continuing to scramble. That would have allowed him to find slot receiver Cody White earlier — who is wide open on the corner route with a coverage bust from Devon Witherspoon — and hit White with some room to run. Regardless, Mendoza eventually finds White and delivers a strike on the run for a 22-yard gain near the sideline.

This is where things started to get a little messy for the rookie, as the play above comes with about a minute left in the second quarter. Mendoza had a few missed opportunities for explosive plays in the game, with the one above being the biggest.

The Seahawks show a two-safety coverage pre-snap but rotate into Cover 3 with the boundary safety spinning down. Since the Raiders are in a three-by-one formation, the boundary corner is locked in man coverage against Tucker, the Silver and Black's best deep threat.

Tucker beats press coverage and has a couple of steps on the corner, while the field or deep safety isn't in a position to help the corner on a pass outside of the numbers. Mendoza gets to Tucker in his progression but doesn't pull the trigger. Maybe left guard Spencer Burford losing control of Murphy discouraged the quarterback from letting it rip, but he has enough time and a clean enough pocket to make that throw.

Instead, Mendoza opts to scramble and only picks up one yard. To be fair, this was the play that had the 4th and 1/first and 10 confusion (see the markers on the field and the scorebug on the top of the screen). So, maybe that played a factor in his decision, but this was certainly a missed opportunity.

Part of the reason Mendoza had such a low EPA is that he took six sacks on the afternoon. That was the result of some great play calling from Seattle's defensive mastermind/head coach Mike Macdonald, and the rookie quarterback's subpar feel for pressure.

This is a perfect play call by Macdonald. Vegas runs a play-action pass where the entire offensive line and the full back work left to sell the run fake, while running back Mike Washington Jr. inserts into the protection scheme on the right edge. Meanwhile, Seattle slants the defensive line to the strong side of the formation as the MIKE and WILL linebackers blitz, on top of the cornerback coming off the edge.

So, the Seahawks end up with a numbers advantage, where the Raiders have three blockers (Zuhn, right tackle DJ Glaze and Washington) to account for four pass-rushers (the edge, two linebackers and the corner). Also, Mendoza's back is turned to the defense, and Kubiak calls a shot play with two deep routes.

That being said, he gets turned around well before Witherspoon gets there and doesn't sense the pressure coming, failing to move off his spot. Again, this is a tough look — especially for a quarterback making his first start.

But if we back up to the pre-snap look, the safety playing outside the hashmark with the corner in press coverage was the tell that Witherspoon was blitzing, especially since the receiver is in a reduced or cut split (meaning between the numbers and hashmark). That creates a learning opportunity for the young QB.

Another sack that highlights how Macdonald tested Mendoza's ability to read the defense when the picture changes post-snap and handle pressure.

Seattle comes out in a double A-gap look with a seven-man front, where four pass-rush threats are on the weak side of the offensive line (in this instance, the offense's right side). So, the quarterback changes the protection, sliding it to the right. However, Macdonald doesn't run a blitz but rather a simulated pressure, where all four defenders on the right side drop into coverage, and the nickel cornerback rushes through the C-gap for a four-man rush.

Since the line is sliding right and there's an edge rusher outside of the tight end, left tackle Kolton Miller stays with Murphy in the protection call, and Mayer sticks with the edge rusher, leaving Witherspoon unblocked as a free rusher. Mendoza doesn't see or feel the pressure and ends up taking another big hit for a sack on third down.

To be fair, it looks like he was trying to throw hot to Ashton Jeanty. But Witherspoon closed so quickly that he had already started making the tackle by the time Jeanty got his head around. Still, this is another instance where Mendoza needs to expect pressure with the nickel as close to the line of scrimmage as he is pre-snap.

Finally, Mendoza had a few throws where his accuracy got away from him, and the one above really hurt.

On this play, it's 2nd and 10 with about 2:45 to play and the Raiders trailing by 11. Seattle keeps the coverage pretty vanilla (for Macdonald's standards, at least), playing Cover 6 with quarters to the wide side of the field near the goal line. Mendoza makes the right read since the safety steps inside while wide receiver Jack Bech runs an out route. That's open for a potential touchdown.

However, Mendoza doesn't finish the throw, so the pass sails on him and falls incomplete. Granted, the pocket is collapsing, but he had enough room to deliver an accurate pass and just missed the opportunity.

If you're looking for more plays from Mendoza's first start, dive into this thread I posted on X/Twitter.

The Chicago Bears may have dropped from the second seed to sixth in the conference standings during their bye, but they are still firmly in the NFC postseason mix at 7-2. And then when the next one comes up, we'll focus on being 1-0 that week. And if you go 1-0 each week and you focus on that each week, you'll turn out to kind of be where you want to be at the end of it."

Head coach Ben Johnson also met the media today, and it's not hard to figure out where Caleb's 1-0 mindset comes from.

When asked about his big-picture take on the NFC North, Johnson said, "It really doesn't matter right now. Once again, it's all about winning this week. So, we'll worry about everything else when it's time. It's not time for that. There's still eight games to be played, and this is the most important one because it's the next one."

The Bears host the Saints on Sunday at Noon.

#2823. Widespread outages hit banks, air travel, the NSA and Apple, disrupting seemingly everything

Director of National Intelligence Jay Clayton dismissed suspicions that malicious activity led to a series of outages at major tech firms and government agencies over the past week.

"This is not cyberattack, and this is not a coordinated effort by a nation state," he told CNBC's "Closing Bell" on Tuesday. "We've looked very closely."

It's been a tough few days for the technology world after a series of widespread disruptions brought different parts of life to a halt.

On Tuesday, an outage halted internal communications at the National Security Agency, and iPhone maker Apple confirmed that different products were either down or having issues.

The NSA pointed to "scheduled routine maintenance," while Apple cited "an internal update" as the reason behind its issues.

It marked four major system problems in four consecutive business days.

Microsoft's Outlook issues wreaked havoc on businesses on Thursday, and the Federal Aviation Administration grounded flights on Friday. On Monday, issues at JPMorgan Chase affected customer transactions, and an outage at United Airlines briefly delayed departing flights.

"When Microsoft goes down, the whole world comes to a standstill," said Raj Rajamani, CEO of JetStream Security. "That's a stark reminder of how deeply embedded these services and technologies are in our day-to-day life."

The disruptions have rattled consumers and led to a steady stream of memes on social media, as people look for a silver lining — or sinister plot — in the string of technology problems.

Experts say the real culprit behind this strange series of coincidences is much more benign.

"We're in the weird teenage phase of technology not quite maturing enough to know how to do this without disrupting services and so forth," said Tom Hegel, research head at SentinelOne.

With the help of artificial intelligence, businesses are patching more software vulnerabilities than ever before and updating archaic systems to prepare for swarms of attacks in the wake of high-profile incidents like Hugging Face.

That's leaving information technology and security teams strained and scrambling to keep up with heightened demand, said Sam Rubin, senior vice president of consulting and threat intelligence at Palo Alto Networks' Unit 42.

Amid this prolonged period of uncertainty, Rubin said businesses should prioritize updating their most exploitable systems and keeping operations underway when an outage hits.

"You need to think about how you continue to operate in the face of one of your IT systems going down," he said.

Clayton told CNBC that routine upgrades are commonplace at any organization and called for a nationwide digital infrastructure upgrade.

Last week, millions of users lost access to email in a fiasco that Microsoft eventually said was the result of an undetected bug that was lurking since July.

The FAA, which is still looking into the root cause of its system failure, pointed in part to a router upgrade in southern Arkansas overseen by government contractor L3Harris Technologies that overwhelmed the agency's telecommunications network.

JPMorgan said longer-than-usual service times on Monday stemmed from a software bug in on-premise hardware systems.

Agencies and businesses have ruled out cyberattacks, but the routine patching alibi hasn't satisfied some skeptics, and lawmakers are calling for more transparency.

Sen. Mark Warner, D-Va., on Tuesday used the recent incidents to make the case for an AI safety bill. House Minority Leader Hakeem Jeffries, D-N.Y., acknowledged that it's "too premature" to conclude malicious activity but called for a congressional briefing.

"I think it's time for the administration to come up to the Hill and explain themselves so we can be informed about the steps that need to be taken and we can inform the people of the United States of America," he said during a press conference on Tuesday.

#2821. James reaches assists milestone, keeps seeing plays ‘before they actually happen'

James reaches assists milestone, keeps seeing plays 'before they actually happen' originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Tyrese Maxey admitted that he wasn't entirely ready for LeBron James' first pass of the afternoon Sunday.

Maxey cut through the paint and James' bounce pass was perfect, but the Sixers' All-NBA guard missed his layup. He's not fully accustomed yet to James' precognitive gifts.

"There's still some times when you're surprised," Maxey said after the Sixers' win over the Pacers. "… I was cutting through just to cut and create space. The ball hit me in the chest and I missed the layup. I was like, 'Ah shoot, my bad.' He was like, 'Yeah, you weren't looking.'"

James has been trying to teach his teammates that he'll spot openings they may not notice.

"I just tell my guys, 'Always pay attention to me when I've got the ball,' because I definitely see plays develop before they happen," James said.

For the most part, James' teammates adhered to his request on the latest historic day in a milestone-filled career. By setting up VJ Edgecombe's layup with 1:19 left in the fourth quarter, James moved into third place on the NBA's all-time assists list, passing Jason Kidd.

The 22-time All-Star recorded 11 assists, his most so far as a Sixer, and now sits at 12,092 in his career. Chris Paul (12,552 assists) and John Stockton (15,806) are the only two players above James.

"It's obviously super humbling," James said. "It's something that I didn't foresee in my career, but I knew assists meant a lot to me. Just the movement of the ball, being able to find my teammates, putting the ball on time and on target.

"And also just being linked with some of the greats that have ever played this game and a dear friend of mine, J-Kidd. It's really special."

James is at or near the top of many all-time leaderboards. He's first in NBA history in points, games and minutes, sixth in steals and fifth in triple-doubles.

Passing has always been a core element of his game.

"First of all, he's got the vision and the awareness," Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said. "He's got the size to see. I think he's got some anticipation. Just a half-second before a play happens, he knows how the defense is moving. … And he throws crisp passes. Some of those are cooking out to the corner three guys, so it doesn't let the close-out get there nearly as quickly."

Whether it's a long-range rocket, a drive-and-kick dish or a cross-court feed from the post that suggests he might have eyes in the back of his head, James has always enjoyed finding a wide range of ways to help his teammates.

"For someone that talented, he's very unselfish," Maxey said. "He really prides himself on getting guys open."

As James recalled Sunday, he had exceptional passing tools long before he entered the NBA 24 seasons ago.

"Since I was a kid, I've had the ability to see plays before they actually happen," he said. "Seeing movement, seeing cracks in the defense before they were even open … it's something that's been with me since I was a kid.

"I just had that vision and it just stuck with me throughout my playing days."

#2819. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Global stock markets remain volatile amid persistent geopolitical tensions and concerns about the sustainability of AI trade. Given this backdrop, investors seeking steady income can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios.

Tracking recommendations from top Wall Street analysts can help investors pick attractive stocks, as these experts assess a company's ability to pay dividends consistently.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is a dividend king. The company has increased its dividend for 39 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share (annualized dividend of $7.12 per share), CVX offers a yield of 3.14%. Chevron will announce its third-quarter results on October 30.

In an earnings preview note on U.S. oil majors, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Chevron with a price target of $241. The analyst is focused on the company's international growth prospects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa, and the Eastern Mediterranean.

Specifically, Mehta highlighted management's commentary on solid execution in Venezuela, with gross production expected to more than double to nearly 600 kbd (kilo barrels per day) by 2031 via three joint ventures. He also noted a recent oil and gas condensate discovery in offshore Angola and is awaiting commercial updates on the West Qurna 2 and Nasiriyah projects in Iraq.

Furthermore, Mehta expects CVX to report improved productivity and capital efficiency across domestic shale assets. He expects the improvement mainly in the Permian Basin, where Chevron projects advanced chemical treatments to drive about a 10% recovery uplift in new wells and bring down capital spend per barrel by about 25% year-over-year. Overall, Mehta expects Chevron to report Q3 2026 EPS of $4.95, above the consensus of $4.89.

"While unfavorable timing effects and volatile commodity prices may present quarterly earnings headwinds, we highlight healthy U.S. refining margins and strong asset utilization as partial offsets," said Mehta.

Mehta ranks No. 381 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Chevron KPIs on TipRanks.

Kinetik Holdings (KNTK) is a fully integrated, pure-play, midstream company operating in the Delaware Basin. Earlier this month, the company declared a dividend of $0.81 per share, payable on October 30, 2026. With an annualized dividend of $3.24 per share, KNTK offers a yield of 5.82%. Kinetik will announce its Q3 earnings on November 4, with a conference call scheduled the next day.

Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, Goldman Sachs analyst John Mackay reiterated a buy rating on Kinetik stock and slightly raised his price target to $58 from $57. He expects the company to report Q3 2026 EBITDA of $279 million, higher than the Street's expectation of $270 million but lower than the second quarter's EBITDA of $281 million.

The 5-star analyst expects Kinetik's Q3 performance to gain from higher gas volumes after notable curtailments in the second quarter. Mackay also expects the quarter to benefit from higher production and steady commodity margins, even though some lower-margin contracts and weaker Waha spread marketing gains could weigh on results.

Overall, Mackay increased his Q3 2026 estimates to reflect higher volumes, driven by rapid Permian egress pipeline commissioning, stronger throughput at Kings Landing I, and higher M2M commodity prices, offset by increased costs.

"As natural gas takeaway constraints ease across the Permian, investor focus centers on KNTK's YE26 volume trajectory and durability of its multi-year growth," said Mackay.

Mackay ranks No. 569 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.9%. See Kinetik's Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Moving to Crescent Energy (CRGY), an exploration and production company with activities focused in the Eagle Ford, Permian, and Uinta Basins. With a quarterly dividend of $0.12 per share (annualized dividend of $0.48 per share), CRGY's dividend yield stands at 3.56%. Earlier this month, Crescent announced an agreement to acquire Eagle Ford assets from Devon Energy (DVN).

Heading into Q3 earnings on Nov. 2, Evercore analyst Stephen Richardson reiterated a buy rating on Crescent stock and slightly lowered the price target to $17 from $18, citing dilution from new shares.

"CRGY's recent acquisition of DVN's Eagle Ford assets marks a strategic, growth-oriented move, potentially enhancing cash flow performance," said Richardson.

The 5-star analyst noted that the acquisition of the Eagle Ford assets at a net purchase price of $3.85 billion will add about 68 MBoe/d of net production and over 600 net Tier 1 locations. Richardson added that a solid track record of synergies from past deals in the Eagle Ford region increases confidence about Crescent achieving efficiencies with this deal.

Additionally, Richardson noted that the financial implications of the acquisition seem impressive, with a projected increase in cash flow per share of about 9% for 2027 to 2029.

The analyst believes that Crescent is not just expanding its business through acquisitions but positioning itself as a more durable company, regardless of market conditions.

Richardson ranks No. 478 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Crescent Energy Statistics on TipRanks.

#2818. IEA chief sees no 'credible pathway' back to pre-war normality in the Strait of Hormuz

The Iran war has "crushed" confidence in the Strait of Hormuz as a reliable supply corridor, IEA Executive Director Fatih Birol warned.

IEA Executive Director Fatih Birol wrote in an article published Wednesday that he was skeptical of a return to "normalization."

"I don't see any credible pathway back to the situation that existed in February 2026," Birol wrote in a guest essay for The Economist's "The World Ahead 2027," published Wednesday.

"Shipping flows through the Strait of Hormuz have been blocked once, with a hugely disruptive effect on oil and gas markets. It can happen again. Confidence in the Strait as a safe and reliable corridor for energy and other goods has been crushed," he said.

Before the war began on Feb. 28, the strait was a major artery for oil and gas. Gulf crude exports have recovered in recent months, reaching more than 80% of pre-war levels in October, the IEA said in its November market report. But renewed U.S.-Iran strikes broke a lull in direct fighting this week.

"I think perceptions of risk around the Strait have changed for good," Birol added, citing new routes, such as pipelines in Saudi Arabia and the United Arab Emirates that bypass the strait.

At 6:45 a.m. ET, U.S. West Texas Intermediate futures were seen trading at $85 a barrel, with international benchmark Brent crude futures at $92.74 a barrel.

Many IEA members relied on emergency oil stocks, softening the economic disruptions, Birol wrote.

Southeast Asian countries such as the Philippines and Thailand are looking to "build up similar buffers," as they were the hardest hit by the energy shock, he added.

Birol predicted that the crisis would accelerate countries' shift toward domestic energy sources such as cost-competitive solar and wind, while renewing interest in nuclear power.

But this will require stronger grids and battery storage, he added.

"Uncertainty is now the rule, not the exception, so energy decision-makers need to be wary of creating new dependencies, even as they seek to address old ones," Birol said.

#2817. Pochettino's USMNT swan song signals change

From the moment Mauricio Pochettino was announced as United States men's national team head coach in September 2024, things felt different. It wasn't his experience or his personality - although, both were certainly noteworthy. Instead, it was his name. His stature. His reputation.

Suddenly, somehow, the United States - the perpetually overlooked, stigmatized name in the halls of global soccer - was dating the prom king.

It was unexpected. It was surreal. And it was bound to be fleeting.

Now, following a 2-0 victory against Haiti at TQL Stadium, in Cincinnati, the Mauricio Pochettino fairytale is officially over. After just two years in charge of the USMNT (and a whirlwind two years at that), Pochettino departs to take up the coveted / cursed position of Manchester United manager.

It's a change that's as disappointing as it is unsurprising. Despite the misty-eyed soundbites over the last week in which he described the decision as "one of the toughest" of his career, Pochettino's return to club football has felt inevitable.

He made no bones about his wandering eye, always quick to profess his love for the Premier League while maintaining his London residence throughout his time with the USMNT. Even when his contract extension was announced this summer, the conditions of that deal made it abundantly clear that should an attractive suitor come calling, he would answer. Now, after years apart, he'll get to rekindle that romance.

As Pochettino celebrated Christian Pulisic putting the U.S. ahead in the 25th minute with one of the most acrobatic goals of the attacker's career (a high-flying bicycle kick that cut the ball inside the far post), some of his personnel were already across the Atlantic, laying the groundwork for his upcoming rendezvous. Even if his couching staff was divided, Pochettino insisted that his focus was not. And with Julian Hall doubling the USMNT's lead in the 43rd minute, perhaps he wasn't lying.

It's rare that football managers exit on their own terms. Even rarer still when they finish out their tenure having already secured their next job. Likely rarest of all, is to go straight from a CONCACAF Nations League quarter-final to the biggest derby in English football.

Then again, little about Pochettino's tenure has been normal.

To even appoint the Argentine, U.S. Soccer aimed higher, spent more, and was bolder than ever before. The mechanisms employed to secure his signature (including donations-as-salary from hedge-fund billionaire Ken Griffin) were as opportunistic as they were canny. Ultimately, they succeeded. But has Pochettino?

If this window is anything to go by then the answer is emphatically yes. Eight goals, zero conceded, across two competitive performances is beyond reproach. Exciting attacks, the promise of youth, undeniable tenacity - much of what fans hoped they would get from Pochettino's appointment was on display against Haiti. But stepping out of the moment, things become a little more complicated.

Pochettino's time as U.S. head coach saw no records broken and no trophies won. The USMNT's ranking is identical to when he took over while his win percentage is less than that of predecessor Gregg Berhalter. Meanwhile, his relationship with the players (Christian Pulisic, in particular) was at times tense, and his rapport with media and fans could even be patronizing. It was, however, also under his watch that 78 different players earned a cap, nine teenagers made debuts, and the USMNT put in one of their all-time great performances (Paraguay), World Cup or otherwise. Altogether, it's a portfolio that plays like a Rorschach test. Love him or hate him, you'll find stats to back you up.

But stats aren't really where Pochettino's legacy lies. Indeed, what Pochettino left the U.S. with - and perhaps even the wider soccer world - was a shift in perception.

The US can should think bigger. Our talent is plentiful. World Cups are winnable.

How true these notions actually are remains to be seen, but what isn't up for debate is who promoted them: Mauricio Pochettino. USMNT head coach Muaricio Pochettino. The sheer reality of that fact makes any future fantasies just a little more plausible. It's something that even Pochettino seemed to recognize. "Being head coach in USA now is prestigious," he said. Self-aggrandizing, sure, but also true - even if only symbolically.

And this, more than anything, is Pochettino's great success.

Two years ago, the thought of a top-tier, globally in-demand manager taking over the United States men's national team was unthinkable. International football simply wasn't a jewel in the managerial crown - let alone a program as disparaged as the U.S. But one Pochettino later, and the narrative has changed.

Now, some of the biggest names in soccer (Tuchel, Carlo, Klopp) find themselves helming national teams (England, Brazil, Germany, respectively) while arguably the biggest name is rumored to be intrigued by the prospect of taking over the USMNT.

The likelihood of Pep Guardiola becoming the next United States head coach is slim, at best. But what's remarkable is that it's no longer laughable.

This is a massive country, with tremendous resources, and enormous potential. The scale and scope of a USMNT "project", combined with the prospect of unleashing a sleeping giant, might actually entice a name as big as Pep (see Man City). Still, in the immediate context of Haiti and a Nation's League quarter final, it's a prospect that's hard to reconcile, and, ultimately, a reflection of the unique place that the U.S. now finds itself.

A place where B.J. Callaghan is mentioned alongside Julian Nagelsmann. Where the self-deprecating backwaters of USMNT fandom have turned a little less dark. Where the once overlooked now get second glances.

A place that was good enough for Mauricio Pochettino.

#2816. Paul George flips the script — and the bird — in Philadelphia return

Paul George flips the script — and the bird — in Philadelphia return originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

PG-13 was rated R in his return to Philadelphia.

The middle finger Paul George directed toward one baseline fan only reinforced that rating.

Tuesday night was expected to revolve around Jaylen Brown and Jayson Tatum sharing an NBA court as opponents for the first time. Instead, the other headliner in the summer's blockbuster trade stole the show.

George scored a game-high 36 points in Boston's 123-101 win over the Sixers. He shot 14 for 22 from the floor, made six of his 11 three-point attempts and scored 14 consecutive Boston points in the fourth quarter.

The game was tied at 79 with 3:19 remaining in the third quarter. Boston outscored Philadelphia 44-22 from that point forward.

The Celtics went nine minutes and 20 seconds without making a two-point field goal during the second half. Instead, they made nine consecutive field goals from three, the second-longest such streak by a Sixers opponent over the last 30 seasons.

Payton Pritchard opened the fourth with consecutive threes. George followed with eight straight points, helping the Celtics begin the quarter on a 14-0 run that turned a competitive game into a rout.

George's return carried a complicated history. His two seasons with the Sixers were repeatedly interrupted by injuries, but he also played a significant role in their most memorable playoff run in years.

Last spring, George helped the Sixers overcome a 3-1 deficit to eliminate Boston in seven games. He averaged 17.4 points and shot 55 percent from three during the series.

Tyrese Maxey was happy to see his former teammate Tuesday, even if he disliked what followed.

"It was good to see Paul," Maxey said. "I love him, love his family. … He gave us everything he had while he was here. We know what he did in the playoffs last year. Great person, great family, and we'll see him again."

Maxey then added one important qualifier.

George instead scored from nearly everywhere.

"He had some open threes. He had some pull-up threes. He had some middies," Maxey said. "He played within the game, too. That's what Paul does. He plays within the game. He takes what the defense gives him, and we gave him too many open looks tonight.

"PG, we know what PG does. We shouldn't even be allowing him to get up 11 threes, and that's on us."

Nick Nurse watched George attack the Sixers in several ways.

"He really had it going," Nurse said. "He'd post up, and-one, fallaways. He went down the lane, high archer off the glass, and-one. He scored in about every way he could tonight."

George heard boos during his return and said the arena carried the atmosphere, though not quite the intensity, of a playoff game.

"This building can be very electric as well," George said. "It was fun. It was fun to play in this environment."

One interaction extended beyond the usual reception for a returning player.

During a stoppage, the ball rolled toward a fan seated along the baseline. George said the fan looked directly at him before dropping the ball instead of returning it.

"There was a fan being an a–," George said. "The ball rolled to him. He looked me dead in my eyes, dropped the ball on the ground as if I'm a dog to go fetch or go get it, instead of passing the ball to me like a normal fan would do.

"And so, yeah, then I rolled him a middle finger in return."

#2814. Senators press acting U.S. Navy Secretary on Iran war costs, sailor health in confirmation hearing

Acting U.S. Navy Secretary Hung Cao's confirmation hearing before the Senate Armed Services Committee led to some heated exchanges as he was grilled about sailors' health and the Iran war.

The Navy changed condition codes on munitions previously classified as expired in order to use them, Cao told senators Tuesday, as lawmakers questioned the conflict's strain on weapons stocks and naval deployments. He also said the military had increased production of key weapons.

"When I was in uniform, we only produced 125 Tomahawks a year. Right now, we're well above 60 every month," he said, adding that the military was working to increase SM-6 missile production and bring in additional ammunition manufacturers.

Cao's confirmation hearing comes as the Republicans, who hold a majority in both the House and Senate, are coming to the end of their time in power in both houses. The Democratic Party flipped many seats in the midterm elections to win a comfortable majority in the next session starting in January. President Donald Trump's nominees for offices, and his legislative agenda, will face much tougher challenges then.

Cao said the Navy was accelerating work on unmanned surface vessels to relieve the 17 destroyers supporting the blockade of Iran's ships, in hopes of reducing the strain on the Fifth Fleet. But Senators still pointed to the war's effect on the Navy's resources.

"For the first time in many years, the United States has no forward-deployed carrier in the Pacific at this moment," Sen. Jack Reed (D-R.I.) noted.

Cao acknowledged that the war was "stressing our naval resources." However, he argued that the demonstration of combined American military capabilities had "really set China back on their heels," a characterization Sen. Mazie Hirono (D-Hawaii) disputed.

Lawmakers also pressed Cao on the war's financial costs, with Cao appearing to agree with Sen. Elizabeth Warren's (D-Mass.) estimate of approximately $32.5 million a day in costs to maintain the blockade on Iranian ports.

The demands of prolonged deployments have raised concerns about sailors' access to food and basic supplies, as Cao told senators that the destroyer USS Gonzalez had a week of rations remaining.

"Usually, I don't like them to go below two weeks of supplies," he said, while insisting the provisions were adequate and ships had never run out of food.

When Sen. Mark Kelly (D-Ariz.) asked about reports of sailors losing 45 pounds, Cao stated that the food preferences might not be to certain sailors' liking.

He likened it to his children opening the refrigerator door and saying "there's nothing to eat here."

"Losing 45 pounds is more serious than just not liking the food. Our sailors are being asked to serve thousands of miles from home for months at a time. We have a responsibility to make sure they have what they need to do their jobs and stay healthy," Kelly posted on X.

Senators also questioned Cao about eight suicide attempts across the USS Abraham Lincoln carrier strike group during its deployment. Cao defended the Navy's mental health support, describing the incidents as "eight suicide mitigations" because personnel had intervened.

Sen. Kirsten Gillibrand (D-N.Y.) called the term "mitigations" an "insult," saying they should be called suicide attempts.

The questioning followed an earlier acknowledgment by the top U.S. commander in the Middle East of mental health concerns aboard the Lincoln.

Cao pledged that the entire Department of the Navy would achieve a clean financial audit by the end of the fiscal year. The Pentagon has had nine consecutive failures to obtain a clean department-wide audit opinion, according to Reuters.

"We owe the American public to tell them exactly where their money is," he said.

If you are having thoughts of suicide, please know that you are not alone. If you are in danger of acting on suicidal thoughts, call 911. If you or someone you know is struggling or in crisis, help is available. Call or text 988 or chat at 988lifeline.org.

(Photo by Ezra Shaw/Getty Images) Getty

The Golden State Warriors are struggling amid the absence of superstar guard Stephen Curry, who is recovering from a right oblique abdominal tear.

Curry missed his second straight game on Monday as the Warriors suffered another blowout loss, this time against the New Orleans Pelicans.

The Warriors will be without Curry until Thanksgiving, though it's possible he could be sidelined for an even longer period, as per BasketNews.

GettySteph Curry #30 of the Golden State Warriors reacts after making a basket against the Los Angeles Lakers at Chase Center on October 30, 2026 in San Francisco, California.

Last season, Stephen Curry missed 39 games last season due to rest, minor issues and a major knee injury. Curry was out for 27 consecutive games after being diagnosed with patellofemoral pain syndrome in his right knee, more commonly known as runner's knee.

Speaking to ESPN's Anthony Slater, Curry shared how painful the injury was and the setbacks he suffered during his adjustment and recovery from what was described as a chronic issue the two-time MVP will have to play with for the rest of his career.

"Against Detroit, it took everything in me down there on the training table just to be like, 'Oh, I can go out here tonight and play,'" Curry said. "And I was just faking it for the first whatever (25 minutes)."

GettySteph Curry #30 of the Golden State Warriors warms up wearing a Golden State Valkyries before their preseason game at Chase Center on October 16, 2026 in San Francisco, California.

The Warriors superstar tried to return after the All-Star break and even worked out with Sabrina Ionescu. However, "there were certain directions of movement and stuff that just didn't work," so his return was pushed back.

Curry was supposed to scrimmage with the team in late March, but his right knee "felt worse than it had before." He called it a "true setback," though he managed to come back before the end of the regular season.

The Warriors labored to make the NBA play-in tournament. They shocked the LA Clippers to have a shot at the No. 8 spot, but they were blown out by the Phoenix Suns.

GettyStephen Curry #30 of the Golden State Warriors stands on the court during their game against the Los Angeles Lakers at Chase Center on October 30, 2026 in San Francisco, California.

In the same interview with ESPN's Anthony Slater, Stephen Curry admitted that the two-year, $115 million extension he signed in the offseason could be his last.

"I assumed that it would probably be one of, if not the last, contract I signed," Curry said. "I'm not afraid to say it. But maybe I'll surprise myself and want to keep going."

At the age of 38, Curry was playing at an MVP level before his oblique injury.

And with the way the Warriors lost their first two games since the injury, they still need their superstar more than ever.

#2812. Iran says army targets U.S. drone and missile sites in Kuwait as Gulf attacks mount

Iran said its army targeted U.S. drone and missile installations at Kuwait's Ali Al Salem air base, according to local media, extending a series of attacks on Gulf states hosting American forces.

Kuwait's army said on Tuesday evening that its air defenses had countered hostile drone attacks, without providing further details.

Tensions between Iran and the U.S. have escalated sharply in recent days as strikes resumed across the Middle East, even as diplomatic talks continued.

Bahrain has also sustained a wave of Iranian drone strikes, issuing an "all-clear" alert on Wednesday after its air defenses intercepted several aerial threats. Iran has previously claimed attacks on U.S. radar systems in Bahrain.

CNN reported late Monday that U.S. intelligence found Iran was developing plans that could involve a so-called dirty bomb to target Washington, D.C. The administration officials, including President Donald Trump, have played down any imminent threat while saying the government continues to monitor the situation.

Speaking at the Association of Southeast Asian Nations summit in Manila, Philippines, Trump warned that Iran would "pay a price the likes of which no country has ever paid before" if such a plan existed.

Secretary of State Marco Rubio echoed Trump's comments when addressing reporters in Manila, saying he did not see any imminent threats to the U.S. capital, but that the government is "always on the lookout for anything that's a threat."

Trump nodded and said, "We are looking into it," while adding, "We are doing very well in Iran."

The renewed attacks come as diplomatic efforts to end the U.S.-Israel war with Iran, now in its ninth month, appear to be stalled, while disruptions continue to weigh on energy flows through the Strait of Hormuz.

Crude futures settled lower amid signs of recovering oil flows through the critical waterway. The International Energy Agency said Friday that the global oil supply had surpassed demand for the first time since the outbreak of the war and projected the market will return to a surplus in the fourth quarter.

The U.S. West Texas Intermediate futures dropped 1.2% to $84.9 a barrel, while Brent was down 0.48% at $92.7 per barrel.

While Barlow remains out with a "lower back stress reaction" listing on the Sixers' injury report, it's one sign that he's trending up in his rehab process.

"He's making progress," Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said. "He was a part of the practice today. It's kind of a game day type of practice … so it wasn't super intense, as you might expect. But he was out there doing some stuff. He's progressing. Just don't know when he'll be ready to go, but at least he's making some progress now."

Barlow was one of the Sixers' top role players last season, starting 59 games and contributing in a variety of areas. He averaged 7.7 points, 4.8 rebounds, 1.2 assists and 0.9 steals in 23.8 minutes per game.

Just historically, we've had a number of guys that have gone down to those games, respected it … and it's really helped them in their careers. … It's been a minute for these guys — both of them — since they've had any real court time.

"So that fit into the schedule well. … I think it will also help them to just find some rhythm, find their legs a little bit. We're going to need everybody at some point, so keep 'em going."

VJ Edgecombe was among the crowd Friday night at Chase Fieldhouse.

#2809. Cincinnati schedules home-and-home with Missouri for 2027 and 2032

While the 2026 regular season is in its twilight stages, some teams are already looking ahead to 2027.

On Tuesday, Cincinnati and Missouri announced a home-and-home non-conference series. Missouri will host the Bearcats at Faurot Field on Sept. 18, 2027, while Cincinnati welcomes the Tigers to Nippert Stadium on Sept. 11, 2032.

Although the campuses are separated by fewer than 500 miles, the 2027 matchup will mark the first-ever meeting between the Big 12 and SEC opponents.

Cincinnati has now finalized its non-conference schedule for the 2027 season. The Bearcats will host Central Connecticut of the FCS on Aug. 28, remain at home against Missouri State on Sept. 4, and complete their non-conference slate at Missouri on Sept. 18. Cincinnati does not have a game scheduled for the weekend of Sept. 11, when it will either play its Big 12 opener or take a bye.

Both Missouri and Cincinnati experienced recent cancelations in their 2027 non-conference slates, making them a perfect match for each other. The Tigers saw their 2027 home contest against Illinois get axed, while Cincinnati's looming road trip to Boston College was also removed from the schedule.

The Bearcats have won five consecutive regular season non-conference matchups, sweeping all three opponents during the 2026 season. The 2027 trip to Missouri will mark the program's first true road non-conference game since Sept. 14, 2024 when it traveled to crosstown rival Miami (OH). Also, it will be the Bearcats' first out-of-state non-conference road game since Sept. 9, 2023 at Pittsburgh and first SEC matchup since Sept. 3, 2022 at Arkansas.

Cincinnati traditionally had one non-conference vacancy reserved for Miami, as the two competed in one of the longest running series in college football. The rivals played their 129th and final scheduled meeting this past September, making it the fourth-most played FBS matchup behind Minnesota-Wisconsin, Auburn-Georgia, and North Carolina-Virginia.

Although an iconic rivalry has been shelved, the Bearcats can look forward to securing their first victory over SEC competition on Sept. 18, 2027 in Columbia.
