#2808. Always one of a kind, Oubre reflects on Sixers stint, why he chose Pacers

Always one of a kind, Oubre reflects on Sixers stint, why he chose Pacers originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Kelly Oubre Jr. experience switched sides Sunday afternoon at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

Back in Philadelphia with the visiting Pacers, Oubre was his usual self. There's never any risk of mistaking Oubre for somebody else.

"Love him," Pacers head coach Rick Carlisle said pregame. "Such an upbeat guy. He's such a unique person. Love the wardrobe, all the different stuff. He's a very authentically one-of-a-kind type guy."

Carlise then detailed Oubre's various basketball qualities, some of which he showed in Indiana's 131-124 loss. The 30-year-old lefty wing scored 16 points and grabbed four rebounds. His day also included a theatrical imitation of Jaylen Brown's off-arm push after the Sixers' star was whistled for an offensive foul.

Sixers fans grew familiiar with Oubre's strengths, weaknesses and eccentricities during his three seasons as a Sixer. He wound up becoming the team's third-longest tenured player behind only Joel Embiid and Tyrese Maxey. Oubre played 178 regular-season games and averaged 14.9 points, 5.4 rebounds, 1.6 assists and 1.3 steals.

After the Sixers replaced president of basketball operations Daryl Morey with Mike Gansey, Oubre signed a two-year contract with the Pacers in free agency.

"Mike came over from Cleveland and I heard whispers that he wanted some of his Cleveland guys," Oubre said before the game in the visitors' locker room. "And I was like, 'OK, cool. I'm a Philly guy. So I guess when he comes over, I will no longer be a Philly guy.' That was kind of the situation at hand.

"No bad blood because business is business at the end of the day. They have a great team over there, and they put together a team that they think is the best for them. I'll be all right elsewhere. I figured it out."

Oubre thought Indiana made sense as his next stop.

"It was the pace, the culture, the attitude that this team has," he said. "It's a fast-paced style of basketball. It's a bunch of guys who work collectively as a unit and kind of figure it out from there.

"I thought that would be a great opportunity for me to come and and kind of free-flow play, not have to think. I was wrong. Been thinking a lot, but I'm starting to figure it out and starting to get my rhythm here. … It's a great culture. Coach Rick Carlisle is a legend, Hall of Fame coach. And they wanted me. I love people who want me."

As Oubre settled into a new city, the Sixers had a stunning, transformational summer.

Gansey traded for Jaylen Brown, signed LeBron James, and added veteran role players like (former Cavalier) Dean Wade, Anfernee Simons and Kentavious Caldwell-Pope. Oubre followed along.

"It was must-see TV, like (for) everybody else," he said. "As a basketball fan, it was pretty cool to see where I was kind of reinvent and get new life and new energy. So I was happy with that. Would I have loved to be a part of it? Of course.

"But at the end of the day, I'm a grown man and I've got to keep it moving and figure it out for myself. It was entertaining. … I'm kind of happy for all the people that have sacrificed over these years. … I'm trying to put a road block in that road for y'all."

Oubre joined the Sixers on a minimum-salary deal in September of 2023 after averaging 20.3 points the past season with the Hornets. Head coach Nick Nurse pitched Oubre on his potential to be a valuable, multi-dimensional player for good teams.

"I was literally about to be done with basketball," he said. "Coach Nurse called me multiple times and said, 'I want you to play for me.' And I was like, 'You know what? This guy really seems to care. He really wants me.' So thank you for just pulling me out of that pit and that hole that I was in four years ago. I got some new life in my basketball career.

"I'm always thankful for that and I love Nurse for that. Just appreciative for my time here. The battle wounds and scars that I've been able to build up, it made me a better man."

Throughout his Sixers stint, Oubre often spoke of growth, evolution, adaptation and self-discovery.

He talked again Sunday about his desire to "Be water" and how life as a Sixer changed him.

"I grew a lot, man," Oubre said. "Just the mental fortitude of being able to withstand adversity. Being able to wake up and not necessarily know which soliders are going to battle, just having to put on different hats on any given night. At first it was challenging, because I'm thinking about all the logistics and things and like, 'This is not what they asked me to do.' But at the end of the day, I just bought into being somebody that can be like water and grow with the team.

"It's been a blessing to be able to play with (VJ Edgecombe), be able to watch Tyrese (Maxey) ascend. Those are the guys where we were front line a lot. Just going through war with those guys was amazing. It just taught me mental fortitude. If I can make it through this, I can make it through anything."

Oubre felt he developed a meaningful connection with the Philadelphia fan base over his three years.

"When they talk about the City of Brotherly Love, I think they're talking about your soul, not necessarily about what you can offer them and what you do," he said. "Everybody in this city works hard. They wake up, go to work, keep the city going. And us as the prima donna basketball players, the least we can do is go hard and sacrifice for them so that they have something to cheer about when they come home from work.

"I think that was the relationship that we had. I'm forever indebted and forever grateful to the people in the city of Philadelphia."

#2807. Robinhood shares fall as crypto slump weighs on profits, offsetting gains in new businesses

Robinhood shares were down around 8% in pre-market trading on Wednesday as investors digested weaker-than-expected third-quarter earnings despite growth in options trading and prediction markets.

Here's how the brokerage performed in its quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with Wall Street estimates, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

Robinhood's net income fell to $468 million, or 51 cents per share, from $556 million, or 61 cents per share, a year ago. Revenue represented a 12% increase from $1.27 billion in the same quarter last year.

Despite that gain, transaction-based revenue of $867 million came in below Wall Street estimates from StreetAccount of $879 million, putting focus on whether Robinhood can sustain momentum across its trading businesses and reduce its reliance on volatile market activity.

Robinhood has been diversifying its business as it tries to expand its brokerage app into a broader financial services platform. Options trading and event contracts have become increasingly important sources of transaction revenue, while the company has continued to invest in wealth management, subscriptions and other offerings.

Transaction-based revenue grew 19% from the same period last year to $867 million, driven primarily by a more than 500% increase in event contracts revenue. Additionally, options revenue grew 31% to $399 million and equities revenue rose 41% to $121 million.

Those gains were partially offset by cryptocurrency revenue, which fell 55% to $120 million.

Chief Financial Officer Shiv Verma told CNBC event contracts on crypto movements did well in the quarter, offering investors another way to express a view on crypto despite the slump in prices and spot trading.

Net deposits on the Robinhood platform totaled $15.7 billion for the quarter, equal to a 17% annualized growth rate based on total platform assets at the end of Q2 2026. Over the past year, net deposits totaled $71 billion, representing 21% growth compared with total platform assets at the end of Q3 2025.

—With reporting from CNBC's Robert Hum and Kate Rooney

#2805. China’s military seeks Nvidia’s advanced chips for AI capabilities, commission says

China's People's Liberation Army has been "actively soliciting" Nvidia's most advanced semiconductor chips to develop its artificial intelligence-enabled military capabilities, a congressional commission said in a report released Wednesday.

The report comes as Nvidia has been rocked by the resignation of its CEO, Jensen Huang, after reports that he had an affair with a suspected Chinese spy who worked for Singapore-based Megaspeed, which is being eyed by U.S. investigators for potential diversion of Nvidia's chips to China.

"China is seeking to evade U.S. export controls to gain the most advanced semiconductors that underpin its intelligentization drive," the U.S.-China Economic and Security Review Commission said in its 2026 annual report to Congress.

China is making that move as it faces a quality gap between chips produced by Chinese companies, including Huawei and Cambricon, and the more advanced ones produced by Nvidia and other U.S. companies, such as Advanced Micro Devices, the report noted.

The report's findings could fuel concerns about advanced Nvidia chips having found their way to Chinese users amid escalating investigations from Congress and state and federal regulators.

"Although China has made some progress in achieving its goals, it has not yet closed the hardest chokepoints — including the most advanced capabilities in logic fabrication, memory, and semiconductor manufacturing equipment, as well as the Nvidia Compute Unified Device Architecture (CUDA) software moat — and likely will not for some time," the report said.

"The resulting gap in compute is substantial, largely due to U.S. export controls on advanced chips," the report said.

The report highlighted the fact that in the meantime, Chinese companies have obtained remote access to Nvidia chips through data centers in third countries, including Malaysia.

On Tuesday, the Commerce Department's Bureau of Industry and Security issued a rule intended to cut off that workaround.

The move came in response to criticism from Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent and Congress members that the Commerce Department had not done enough to prevent Nvidia's most advanced chips from being diverted to China in violation of U.S. export controls.

The U.S.-China Economic and Security Review Commission was created by Congress in 2000 with a mandate to monitor and report to Congress the national security implications of the trade and economic relationship between the U.S. and China.

#2804. Is Cricket Australia re-opening investigation into Sandpapergate? CA announces new 'forum' as Usman Khawaja expresses concern

Is Cricket Australia re-opening investigation into Sandpapergate? CA announces new 'forum' as Usman Khawaja expresses concern originally appeared on Cricket News. Add Cricket News as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

Cricket Australia has set up a new forum linked to the 2018 Cape Town incident.

Usman Khawaja's book triggered the move, and several former colleagues have rejected his claims.

The board insists this is not a fresh inquiry, yet Khawaja still has doubts.

Cricket Australia has stepped back into one of the most uncomfortable chapters in its recent history, and the way it has done so has left many people asking what the board is really trying to achieve. The governing body announced on Wednesday that it will create a forum for those connected with the 2018 sandpaper incident in South Africa.

Interim chair David Boon and chief executive Todd Greenberg made the announcement at Bellerive Oval, a little more than an hour before the third ODI between Australia and England was due to begin.

The board had met the night before and agreed on the plan, which allows players and staff who were involved in the events of that tour to share their experiences.

The decision comes only days after Usman Khawaja made a series of allegations about the Cape Town incident in his autobiography, Sabr, which was published last week. His claims drew responses from captain Pat Cummins and others, and they have kept a subject that many in Australian cricket hoped was behind them in the headlines.

MORE: Former Aussie star reconfirms everyone knew about Sandpapergate

According to the board, the forum will be confidential, and it will be open to anyone who was present at the time, although nobody will be forced to take part. An independent person will be appointed in the coming days, along with the terms of reference, and CA will decide later how much of the process is made public.

Boon stressed that the aim is not to go back over the incident but to let those involved share their recollections as part of a push to move forward.

"Last night, the Cricket Australia Board met, and we reached a decision that we believe is in the best interests of the game," Boon said. "After careful discussion, we agreed to establish a forum that will allow players and staff involved in the events of South Africa in 2018 to share their experiences and their perspectives."

The interim chair then described how the long-running debate has affected the sport, which he said has become a distraction over time.

He added: "For almost a decade, the fallout from these events, or from those events, has remained part of the conversation around Australian cricket. It has been a difficult chapter for many people, and increasingly, the ongoing debate has become a distraction for our game. We believe it is time for a circuit breaker."

Boon repeated that the forum is not intended to revisit the past in a legal sense, and he closed with a call for unity.

"This forum is not about re-litigating the past at all. It's about creating an opportunity for those who wish to be heard to tell their stories in a respectful, safe environment, and for our sport to listen."

"Australian cricket is at its strongest when we're united, and we owe it to everyone who loves the game to make every reasonable effort to move forward together with understanding, respect, and perspective."

The short answer from the board is no. Greenberg confirmed that the original 2018 investigation will neither be reopened nor made public, with confidentiality given as the reason.

Greenberg said the game is hurting right now, and then spoke about the need to recover.

"It's not good for anyone. We need to find a path through, and we need some time to heal."

He also made clear what the new process is and is not meant to do, and he repeated that taking part is a personal choice.

He said: "As part of this process, and so that all parties who wish to can tell their version of events, we have set up now an independent forum. It's a chance to give everyone a voice in an independent and a safe setting. It's not a re-investigation. It's not designed to re-prosecute or to sanction."

"No one is compelled to participate. It is entirely up to each individual. It's important to remember we're talking about events that happened eight and a half years ago, and I hope everyone will respect this process."

MORE: Warner accused CA of never interviewing bowlers in Sandpapergate probe, clarifies statement

Khawaja welcomed the idea of a safe space but told Kayo Sports that he still has questions about the forum. He wants the public to hear the full story, and he believes that is impossible unless every person involved speaks.

He said: "I have always wanted a safe space to chat… I also have some questions about Cricket Australia because the public wants to know what happened there. It's hard to get the whole truth unless everyone is speaking."

MORE: Akram questions Australian bowlers' 'didn't know' defence in Sandpapergate

He then said he does not want to see anyone punished, and he raised a concern about what the board will do with the material.

He further asked: "I don't think there should be any sanctions… the board makes the decisions but what comes out at the end? I have a really good relationship with Todd Greenberg, and I respect what he's trying to do there.

"But at the end of the day if you're going to cherry-pick what is said in this report, the board can choose whatever they want to come in, or come out. Are we just going in a circle here? That's the thing I'm not sure about."

Warner, who was speaking alongside Khawaja, also ruled out any desire for further penalties. He pointed to what the players accepted at the time and to what he sees as the real purpose of the forum.

He said: "We put our hands up, we accepted what Cricket Australia handed out to us. It's not about penalties. It's about transparency and accountability - which Todd's acknowledged, on CA's behalf, about the culture at the time with the players and CA."

MORE: 'Tried eradicating it': Cummins admits Australia of 2018 had a 'win at all costs' mentality

The board's intention is easy to understand, because nearly a decade of argument has simply worn people down. A confidential, voluntary forum offers a way for everyone to speak without the threat of sanctions, and that is a reasonable starting point.

However, the same features that make the forum safe may also limit its value. Khawaja has already pointed out the central weakness: CA will decide what is released at the end. If the public sees only a short summary, those who want answers are likely to treat it as a polished version of events, and the debate will carry on.

The voluntary nature of the process is a second problem. Cummins and Starc have strongly rejected Khawaja's version, and if they choose not to take part, the forum will hear one side of the dispute far more than the other. A discussion with missing voices is unlikely to produce the shared understanding Boon hopes for.

CA can improve its chances by publishing the terms of reference early, by naming a respected independent figure, and by being clear about what will be shared. The board has said it wants a circuit breaker, but a breaker only works if people trust it. That trust will depend on how openly the process is run.

#2803. Observations after Sixers shake off slow start, Embiid injury scare to win

Observations after Sixers shake off slow start, Embiid injury scare to win originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers brushed off their blowout loss to the Celtics and got back in the win column Thursday night.

They overcame a first-quarter deficit as high as 17 points and earned a 123-112 home win over the Raptors. The Sixers moved to 10-3 and Toronto fell to 5-7.

Tyrese Maxey had 27 points and eight assists. Joel Embiid added 20 points.

Jaylen Brown tallied 18 points and six rebounds. VJ Edgecombe posted 15 points, eight rebounds and a season-high eight assists. Anfernee Simons scored 17 points and shot 5 for 6 from three-point range.

RJ Barrett recorded 31 points and 14 rebounds. Immanuel Quickley had 26 points and six assists.

The Sixers were without LeBron James (low back/sciatica management), Adem Bona (cervical spine sprain), Dominick Barlow (lower back stress reaction) and Ariel Hukporti (right Achilles tendon tear).

The team told reporters Thursday morning that Bona will be re-evaluated in approximately one week and that Barlow participated in shootaround. The 23-year-old forward also went through a pregame workout on the Xfinity Mobile Arena floor. There's no formal timetable for his return.

Toronto's sidelined players included Kawhi Leonard and Scottie Barnes.

The Sixers will face the Pacers on Sunday afternoon in Philadelphia. Here are observations on their victory over the Raptors:

Dean Wade began the game in James' normal spot. The Sixers started slowly, missing their first foul field goals and opening 1 for 8 from three-point range.

He stayed down after setting a ball screen and having an apparent knee-to-knee collision with Ja'Kobe Walter. The Sixers called timeout with 8:30 left in the first quarter and Embiid grabbed at his right knee. He eventually rose with assistance from teammates and Sixers head athletic trainer Kevin Johnson, then limped back to the locker room. Jabari Walker entered.

At the 5:26 mark of the first quarter, Embiid checked back in to cheers from the home fans.

"I knew that he didn't twist it or anything," Maxey said. "I knew it was a knee-to-knee, so I wasn't too worried. He kept saying, 'I just got kneed.' That still hurts, though."

Embiid ended up logging 28 minutes, though he certainly wanted to play more.

"He was very, very motivated to play," Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said. "It was hard to get him out for his scheduled rests tonight, which is a good problem sometimes."

While Embiid's return was a major positive development, the Sixers fell further behind. The Raptors' lead swelled to 32-16 with a Jamison Battle three-pointer and Nurse burned a timeout.

Simons sunk a buzzer-beating three to conclude the first quarter, cutting the Sixers' deficit to nine points.

"Those are big momentum plays that usually have some weight to them and carry you into the next moment," Nurse said.

The Sixers kept rolling against a shorthanded Toronto team playing on the second night of a back-to-back. Simons nailed a triple to kick off the scoring in the second quarter. Brown buried a mid-range jumper and Edgecombe jammed in a crowd-pleasing dunk to give the Sixers a 35-34 edge.

Brown and Edgecombe shined in the second quarter both as individuals and as a duo. The Sixers need Edgecombe to attack a bit more offensively when he shares the floor with Brown and bench players. It doesn't hurt for the second-year guard to have that clarity about his role in those units.

"He's one of our better playmakers," Maxey said of Edgecombe. "He's really good at getting downhill and creating for himself and others."

The Sixers' only three bench rotation players were Walker, Simons and Kentavious Caldwell-Pope (11 points, five assists). In addition to Walker, Wade got center minutes behind Embiid.

Embiid picked up his third foul with 2:23 left in the second quarter and had to take a seat. The teams were tied at 60 apiece through two periods.

The game remained even for much of the third, but Maxey fueled an 11-0 Sixers run late in the quarter.

His speed and the Sixers' collective focus on pushing the pace whenever possible allowed the team to get regular points before Toronto could set its defense. Maxey zoomed in for a layup, then tossed the ball ahead to Brown for an easy bucket. Brown assisted a Simons three. Walker converted a fast-break lay-in and canned a three on the first possession of the fourth quarter. All of a sudden, the Sixers held a 101-87 lead.

Brown, Edgecombe and the bench gave the Sixers more good minutes early in the fourth quarter. They sealed the deal without any late drama. And the fans chanting for a Tacko Fall appearance got their wish with a final-minute entrance by the 7-foot-6 center.

#2802. Worried about the AI bubble popping? How investing pros say to adjust your strategy

Recent headlines coming out of the technology sector may have some investors feeling queasy.

Shares of Nvidia, the firm whose chips have powered much of the artifical intelligence boom, are down 35% since their high on October 6. Part of the downdraft came after company co-founder Jensen Huang resigned as CEO amid revelations of his romantic ties to a woman believed by the U.S. intelligence community to be a Chinese spy.

All told, tech companies in the S&P 500 are down nearly 17% from their early October highs, which has many investors wondering: Is the AI bubble we've all been hearing about finally ready to pop?

"People have been worried for awhile that we have a tech-heavy, top-heavy market that's overly focused on a single theme," says Dan Lefkovitz, a strategist with Morningstar Indexes. "They've drawn parallels to the dotcom era and railroads and every other transformative technology build-out we've seen."

Those concerns aren't likely to dissipate anytime soon. The 10 largest companies in the S&P 500 currently command 37% of the index, and 37% of the benchmark's weighting is in tech stocks as of Nov. 17. That is before Anthropic's initial public offering, which could add another major AI player to the top of the market. While the firm won't immediately join the S&P 500, it's reportedly targeting a valuation that would make it the tenth-largest publicly traded U.S. firm on the market when it begins trading.

A market that's top-heavy and heavily tilted toward a single industry doesn't a bubble make. Historically, market bubbles have come when the price investors will pay for a particular investment becomes disconnected from its underlying value — and the jury is still out on whether that's the case for major AI stocks.

But if the idea that a major chunk of your portfolio revolves around a single technology is keeping you up at night, it may make sense to adjust your strategy, financial experts say. Here's how.

If you're worried about a potential bubble bursting, your first step is to evaluate your portfolio's exposures, says Anthony Saglimbene, chief market strategist at financial services firm Ameriprise.

"You want to make sure you're diversified, and that not all of your eggs are in tech or the AI theme," he says, adding that, after years of outperformance, tech firms make up a huge portion of many passively managed, market-capitalization-weighted index funds. "That works well when tech is going well. It doesn't work right now when tech is having a sell-off."

If you determine you want to dial back your exposure to AI and technology names, however, the smart move isn't to take your ball and go home, says Lefkovitz. Rather, he says, you'd be wise to maintain your exposure to stocks while broadening what your portfolio holds.

"Exiting the market is an extreme option," he says. "People have been talking about the market being overvalued or too tech-heavy, too AI-heavy for years now. And if you had exited, you would have missed out on some tremendous gains."

So consider tweaking your portfolio at the margins, he suggests. That may mean adding exposure to an equal-weight version of your favorite index, rather than one that holds bigger companies in bigger quantities. You could also tilt towards indexes that track international stocks or smaller U.S.-based companies — both of which are less exposed to tech and AI names. The same general idea applies to dividend strategies and ones that focus on undervalued stocks, which tend to skew toward older-economy firms.

No matter which route you consider, be sure to talk with a trusted financial advisor before making any wholesale moves in your portfolio. The goal, says Lefkovitz, isn't to upend your strategy or abandon AI or tech altogether, but to dial things down to a level that you're comfortable with.

"There are ways to participate [in the stock market] without being so reliant on the top end of the market and that AI theme," he says.

You could examine your portfolio and find that you're entirely comfortable with your exposure to AI. Even with tech in turmoil, many market watchers see a persistent demand for AI infrastructure, such as microchips and data centers, and have faith that so-called hyperscalers — megacap tech firms such as Alphabet and Microsoft — will reap huge future profits from the current AI buildout.

While the current environment presents the risk of a short-term drawdown, younger investors hoping to cash in on AI should focus on the long-term opportunity the technology presents, says Jamie Cox, managing partner for financial planning firm Harris Financial Group.

"If you're Gen Z and you're worried about a tech bubble, you're not thinking about this right," he says. Those with decades to invest should be comfortable with the short-term ups and downs of the stock market because they have plenty of time to let fallen markets recover and ascend higher, he says: "Time is on your side."

Historically, the U.S. stock market has trended upward, rewarding investors who were willing to buy and hold stocks. That's not always an easy proposition in the short term, notes Ryan Detrick, chief market strategist at Carson Group, a wealth management firm. Broad-market pullbacks of 5% or more happen more than three times per year, on average, with drawdowns of 10% or more happening about once a year, he notes.

"Every year you're going to have volatility, and it's uncomfortable. But volatility is the toll we pay to invest," he says. "If you want the joys of longer-term stock returns over decades, you're going to have some very uncomfortable moments along the way."

#2801. Lions create practice squad spot, set stage for Wednesday roster moves

The Detroit Lions announced on Tuesday that they've released defensive lineman Ben Stille from the practice squad, creating a vacancy on the 17-man squad ahead of their week of preparation for the Tampa Bay Buccaneers.

Stille, a 29-year-old veteran defensive lineman, was a standout during training camp this offseason, but he failed to make the 53-man roster. He was quickly signed to the practice squad and got the call-up for Detroit's Week 1 game, playing just nine defensive snaps.

This move sets the stage for what will likely be a few roster moves on Wednesday. How the Lions will fill the practice squad spot remains to be seen, but it is certainly worth noting that on Monday, the Lions waived cornerback Nick Whiteside to make room for waiver claim DE Tyrus Wheat. On Tuesday, Whiteside cleared waivers, so if Detroit wanted to bring Whiteside back, there's now a spot on the practice squad for that. Whiteside spent two months on the Lions' practice squad earlier this year.

On top of that, the Lions are expected to activate safety Kerby Joseph from the Physically Unable to Perform list to the 53-man roster on Wednesday, the final day of Joseph's 21-day evaluation window. If the Lions don't activate Joseph, he would be shut down for the year, but on Tuesday, coach Dan Campbell indicated that Joseph will not only be activated, but they expect him to have a significant role in Sunday's game against the Buccaneers.

"The plan is to get him going. I think we're going to let him play," Campbell said.

It's not clear how the Lions will create the roster spot for Joseph, but the easiest way would be to place Aidan Hutchinson on injured reserve after he suffered a torn ACL on Sunday.

#2800. Ohio State Drops Concerning Injury Update on RB Ja’Kobi Jackson

COLUMBUS, OHIO - SEPTEMBER 26: Julian Sayin #10 of the Ohio State Buckeyes hands the ball off to Ja'kobi Jackson #24 during the first quarter of the game against the Illinois Fighting Illini at Ohio Stadium on September 26, 2026 in Columbus, Ohio. (Photo by Kirk Irwin/Getty Images)

AUSTIN, TEXAS - SEPTEMBER 12: Ja'kobi Jackson #24 of the Ohio State Buckeyes runs the ball for a touchdown against Justin Cryer #28 of the Texas Longhorns during the first quarter at Darrell K Royal-Texas Memorial Stadium on September 12, 2026 in Austin, Texas. (Photo by Alex Slitz/Getty Images)

The Ohio State Buckeyes thought they'd have a two Jackson backfield to carry them this season.

Bo Jackson remains healthy, but Ja'Kobi Jackson remains out with an injury. It's not as important an absence as that of QB Julian Sayin, but it isn't an ideal situation for OSU heading into the last two games of the regular season.

Ohio State plays at Nebraska on Saturday before returning home to host Michigan on Saturday, Nov. 28.

The odds are slim of the Buckeyes making the College Football Playoff, but to do it, they'll at the very least need to win both of their remaining regular season games.

It doesn't sound like Ja'Kobi Jackson will be part of the picture to do that, though.

GettyCOLUMBUS, OHIO – SEPTEMBER 26: Julian Sayin #10 of the Ohio State Buckeyes hands the ball off to Ja'kobi Jackson #24 during the first quarter of the game against the Illinois Fighting Illini at Ohio Stadium on September 26, 2026 in Columbus, Ohio. (Photo by Kirk Irwin/Getty Images)

Jackson remains out after having foot surgery in October.

Ohio State head coach Ryan Day held his weekly press conference on Tuesday and provided an update, although it wasn't a good one.

He said that once Jackson begins running again, the Buckeyes will then consider him "week-to-week" — but added that Jackson hasn't even started running yet.

With time running out on the season, it's seeming less and less likely that Ja'Kobi Jackson will play again for Ohio State this season.

GettyAUSTIN, TEXAS – SEPTEMBER 12: Ja'kobi Jackson #24 of the Ohio State Buckeyes runs the ball for a touchdown against Justin Cryer #28 of the Texas Longhorns during the first quarter at Darrell K Royal-Texas Memorial Stadium on September 12, 2026 in Austin, Texas. He has peaked in workload in the three games before he was hurt.

Jackson ran 10 times for 56 yards and a score against Illinois, then 14 times for 72 yards and a TD at Iowa, before getting 14 carries for 76 yards (and two catches for eight yards) against Maryland.

That Maryland game was the last one Jackson played in up to this point of the season.

And unless Ohio State manages a deep playoff run against all odds, Jackson might already have to start looking toward next season.

#2799. Jerry Jones makes surprising statement about 49ers takeover

One of the biggest shockers from the 49ers' 46-40 road victory against the Cowboys on Sunday was the takeover of AT&T Stadium by San Francisco fans.

Cowboys owner Jerry Jones appeared on "105.3 The Fan" on Tuesday morning and was asked his reaction to 49ers fans filling up his stadium and said, "That was not a shocker."

Jerry Jones downplays 49ers fans taking over AT&T Stadium on Sunday. AP Photo/Jeff Roberson

"Well, I don't know if you saw the price of those tickets that they were paying … but it was unbelievable," Jones said. "I would say the highest price of any ticket."

Jones said the 49ers have "always had a way above-average attendance to our away games here in Dallas" and that longtime Cowboys season ticket holders blamed "this secondary market."

Jerry Jones meeting with members of the media following Sunday's game against the 49ers. AP Photo/Jeffrey McWhorter

"Many of our fans have seats licensed. So they technically own those seats," Jones said. "For the most part, fans that have been out there for years, and years, and years. A lot of times, our ticket holders will maybe take two or three key games of the year and sell those tickets."

While Jones insisted that Cowboys fans price-gouged 49ers fans for their tickets, Dallas has certainly enjoyed a large turnout by its fans this season.

According to ESPN, Dallas ranks first in the NFL for attendance, averaging 91,766 fans per home game. San Francisco averages 71,131 fans per home game.

After the game, Jones was asked about the 49ers fans' invasion and gave a defensive response: "We have a lot of [Cowboys fans] on the road ourselves."

San Francisco 49ers fans seen filling up AT&T Stadium during Sunday's game. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

That is accurate, too. ESPN reported that Dallas has averaged 73,450 fans per road game.

Cowboys quarterback Dak Prescott was also aware of the sea of red in the seats and told reporters he "[t]ricked myself, told myself early the red was for my high school."

Prescott applauded the 49ers Faithful. "Their fans were more present than ours. But it created a great atmosphere, so take the good out of it one way or another. Leave it there."

San Francisco's fans impacted Prescott's offense, forcing Dallas to utilize a silent count.

#2798. Ukraine allies reject U.S. criticism of deep strikes inside Russia ahead of Trump-Putin-Xi summit

Germany and the U.K. have pushed back against U.S. criticism of Ukraine's deep strike campaign inside Russia, underscoring tensions between Washington and European allies over Kyiv's war strategy.

The clash comes ahead of a highly anticipated trilateral meeting on Thursday between U.S. President Donald Trump, Russian President Vladimir Putin and Chinese President Xi Jinping on the sidelines of the Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation forum in Shenzhen, China.

Geopolitical flashpoints such as trade, artificial intelligence and nuclear weapons are expected to be a major focus of Trump, Putin and Xi's meeting, with external observers closely monitoring any discussion of Russia's full-scale invasion of Ukraine.

Speaking at NATO's headquarters in Belgium on Tuesday, U.S. Undersecretary of Defense Elbridge Colby likened Ukraine's deep strikes inside Russia to a spectacle, adding that they "have not been effective in driving an end to the war, nor do they contribute to blunting Russia's advances on the front line."

The Pentagon policy chief acknowledged Ukraine's mid-range strike campaign using novel drone technologies and tactics to deny Russian advances on the front line has proven "very effective."

He added that Ukraine's Armed Forces gained more territory than Russia for the second consecutive month in October.

However, Colby said Ukraine's deep strikes targeting "civilian infrastructure," citing Russia's biggest online retailer Wildberries, "have actually probably hardened the Russian public's resolve and the Kremlin's own willingness to escalate."

Kyiv has said that warehouses targeted by Ukrainian drones were involved in providing Russian forces with drone components, navigation equipment and other military components. Wildberries and the Kremlin have denied the company is involved in Russia's war in Ukraine.

Asked whether he shared Colby's position on Ukraine's deep strike campaign inside Russia, German Defense Minister Boris Pistorius said criticizing strikes designed to destroy strategic military and military-use targets in Russia "is quite a stupid thing, I think."

"Instead of criticizing that, we should rather criticize and talk about the terroristic strikes every night and every day on civil energy, water and heating infrastructure in Ukraine. This is kind of terrorism," Pistorius said at a news conference.

British Secretary of State for Defence Wes Streeting echoed those comments, saying it is "not for us to tell Ukraine how to fight their war."

He added: "There is no doubt in my mind that it has helped to degrade Putin's war machine, buying time and saving lives."

Trump has previously called on Ukraine to halt strikes on Russian oil refineries and infrastructure used to produce and distribute diesel, saying the attacks were responsible for a global shortage.

Ukraine has stepped up its strikes on Russian oil facilities and logistics infrastructure in recent months, seeking to raise the cost of the war for Moscow after more than 4½ years of conflict.

Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelenskyy on Tuesday lauded an "unprecedented operation" targeting the Kerch Bridge linking Russia to occupied Crimea in what marked a symbolic blow to the Kremlin's war effort.

A coordinated drone and missile strike on the bridge, which provides a road and rail route for Russian forces and is one of Putin's signature projects, damaged the crossing.

In response, Russia said the bridge would be restored and "will be even better than before."

#2797. You’ve Been Serb’d: Illinois 99, Duke 78

First of all, there's no doubt that Illinos deserved the win at Cameron on Tuesday night. The Illini just thumped the Blue Devils in almost every way. They outrebounded Duke, 54-34, and Duke had 14 turnovers. Illinois also outscored Duke 31-21 from the line, and ruthlessly controlled the paint. Duke took 40 threes in this game, hitting just 9. Illinois shot just 6-32, but their control of the paint made that irrelevant. And so did the performance of Andrej Stojakovic.

When Duke played Cal in 2024, Stojakovic finished with 10 points on 4-14. Tuesday night, he lit Duke up for 46 points on 14-21/2-3 from the floor, along with 16-20 from the free throw line.

It was a record-breaking performance: the most points ever scored by an opponent in Cameron was 43 by South Carolina's Grady Wallace in 1957, when the ACC was 3 years, 9 months, and 15 days old.

It came perilously close to breaking Dick Groats' record of 48 points, set in 1952.

He was superb and nearly unguardable, but he's not the only reason that Illinois won. Quentin Coleman racked up 27, but it was more than that.

Here's something you may not have caught: Zavier Zens, cousin of Kon and Kager Knueppel, who is 6-7, got 8 rebounds. He out-rebounded every Duke player who got on the floor. So did Coleman, who is 6-4, and so did reserve Stevan Vaaks, who, like Zens, is 6-7.

JoJo Boumtje Boumtje got 5 rebounds. He's 7-2. Patrick Ngongba got 4 rebounds. He's 6-11. Cameron Williams, 7-0, got 3. Drew Scharnowski, 6-10, got 2.

Three players, all 6-7 or under, and two of them freshmen, outrebounded Duke's stable of big men. The leading rebounder for Duke?

Basically, the same thing happened with Illinois that happened at Michigan State: both teams were significantly tougher and more focused than was Duke, and the Blue Devils paid the price.

At the half, Scheyer pulled two starters: Boumtje Boumtje and Cayden Boozer, opting for Foster and Sebastian Wilkins instead.

Wilkins has played very well all season, and deserved his shot, and Foster is highly reliable, but it didn't make any difference, because Stojakovic stayed red hot, and to a lesser extent, so did Coleman.

Those two finished with 73 of the 99 Illinois hung up in Cameron.

It was a dismal night, but nonetheless, there were some bright moments. You could argue that Duke's second five outplayed the starters. Cameron Williams came in and almost immediately blocked two shots. That's at least the second time he's done that, and possibly the third. He has great instincts for blocks.

Scharnowski hit a three, which, we believe, was the first he's ever hit in college. He played hard and certainly didn't back down. Deron Rippey didn't score, but he had 7 assists to two turnovers. And when Bryson Howard was in, he may have defended Stojakovic more effectively than anyone else on the team. He really gave him trouble in a way no one else did.

We've already talked about Wilkins and Foster, but we should say this about Wilkins: he's looking better and better. He moves incredibly well on the court, he defends, and he's the furthest thing from selfish.

We're not sure what lessons Jon Scheyer will draw from this game, but from our vantage point, we saw that Duke was soft inside. And until that gets fixed, there are going to be games like this.

We've seen enough of Ngongba to know that a bad game doesn't mean the next one will be bad, too, but we can't say that about Boumtje Boumtje.

Clearly, he's got a perimeter game, but 7-2 is a gift from God that one shouldn't squander. In the second half, he was far more willing to take it to Illinois, and got to the line as a result. At whatever point he gets mad enough to rip someone's head off on a dunk, you'll know that part of the problem with this team has been solved.

Side note: remember when Dahntay Jones did pushups after that insane dunk against Virginia?

The reason he did it was because he was angry at his coach, Mike Krzyzewski, who had challenged him in some pretty elemental ways, as we understand it, and Jones wanted to make a point.

We're not sure how Scheyer will do that, but it might need to be done.

And in fairness to Boumtje Boumtje, and we're not picking on him, but the kid is 17. He's adapting to a very new situation. Let's give them both time and space to figure that out.

The reality though is that in sports, there's a goal, and that goal is to win. As we saw in the second half, sometimes changes need to be made. It's going to be hard not to give minutes to Williams, Scharnowski, Howard, and most of all, Wilkins, if the starters let themselves get pushed around.

Notes – Scheyer got a technical in the first half, which we think is his third as head coach…so did Dame Sarr…people are upset about the loss, but remember that Coach K took some beatings during his long and storied career…we wouldn't judge this team in November…not only was that Scharnowski's first made three, but it looks like it's the only one he's taken in three years…we'd love to see Blackwell go off soon…

#2796. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long haul

The ongoing earnings season, Middle East tensions and buzz ahead of the U.S. midterm elections have been keeping investors busy. Further, worries about elevated artificial intelligence spending are weighing on investor sentiment.

Investors looking beyond the short-term noise and focusing on long-term returns can track top Wall Street analysts to pick the right stocks, backed by strong research.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first stock pick is Datadog, an AI-powered observability and security platform. The company will announce its results for the third quarter on Nov. 4. Datadog is seeing strong demand for its offerings, thanks to cloud migration and rapid AI adoption. TipRanks' KPIs tool for DDOG shows the growth in its customer base over the past few years amid increasing market penetration and growing adoption of its products.

Heading into Q3 FY26 earnings, Bank of America analyst Koji Ikeda reiterated a buy rating on Datadog stock and raised his price target to $335 from $305. The analyst noted that industry checks and data indicate that infrastructure software demand is strengthening, with AI increasingly driving usage and growth.

Ikeda called Datadog and Atlassian his top picks, saying that companies that can show that AI is a tailwind to growth could see their valuation rerate, even at premium multiples. He added that the total addressable market for observability is growing faster than anticipated, driven by accelerated growth of enterprise AI and the rapid ramp of consumer AI agents.

The five-star analyst believes that focus will be on revenue growth, which he estimates to be 28.7% for Q3 FY26 versus the Street's consensus of 29.1%. Ikeda thinks that the market is underestimating the growth prospects of DDOG's security products. Meanwhile, he cautioned investors about concentration risk related to large customers.

"We think results will be supportive of our long-term thesis that Datadog is a strong share gainer in the rapidly expanding observability opportunity," said Ikeda.

Ikeda ranks No. 673 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 55% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.9%. See Datadog Statistics on TipRanks.

The next pick, semiconductor giant Nvidia, is also benefiting from AI-driven demand for its products. After attending Nvidia's GTC Berlin conference, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reaffirmed a buy rating on NVDA stock with a price target of $300, calling it a top pick in the semiconductor space.

The five-star analyst said that the conference supported his thesis that Nvidia continues to effectively navigate bottlenecks in the supply chain, land/power/shell, and funding. In fact, Moore is "convinced that all 3 aspects are opportunities for NVDA."

While Nvidia's GTC Berlin offered no major updates, Moore noted the conference reflected that the company continues to add to its significant advantages heading into 2027. The analyst said that Vera Rubin is gaining momentum, though details on next year's Rubin Ultra were lighter than expected.

With demand outpacing supply, Moore said that focus is on closing the gap. In this regard, he highlighted the supply chain deals on substrate, packaging and testing. While the analyst continues to view TSMC 3 nm wafer supply as the biggest challenge, he expects it to ease over time.

Moore ranks No. 142 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.4%. See Nvidia Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Aerospace company SpaceX recently announced solid third-quarter results, with its AI revenue surging to $7.56 billion from $813 million in the prior-year quarter.

Impressed by the results, Needham analyst Ryan Koontz reiterated a buy rating on SPCX stock with a price target of $250. Koontz highlighted that SpaceX's Q3 results significantly exceeded expectations, proving that the company's vertically integrated business model is driving massive scale.

Furthermore, Koontz emphasized the 195.2% sequential growth in SpaceX's AI revenue, fueled by wholesale spot compute deals. He noted management's increasing focus on its AI strategy, given that it is optimistic about adding 10 gigawatts of incremental capacity during 2027.

"With increasing confidence on Starship re-use, we meaningfully increase our estimates," said Koontz.

Koontz ranks No. 357 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 42% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.7%. See SpaceX Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2795. How LeBron James dissected Pacers defense down the stretch

How LeBron James dissected Pacers defense down the stretch originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

As he overtook Jason Kidd for the third-most assists in NBA history, LeBron James piloted the Sixers' offense down the stretch against the Indiana Pacers on Sunday.

With Joel Embiid sitting out and Tyrese Maxey getting a breather on the bench, James took over in the fourth quarter. He did so by spamming the same action over and over again.

The set consisted of James getting a ball screen from a guard – either VJ Edgecombe or Anfernee Simons – at the top of the key. After setting or ghosting the screen, the guard would short roll towards the basket.

When the Pacers blitzed or hedged the screen, James found the guard. The Sixers could then attack a 4-on-3 or 3-on-2 situation.

Twice, Edgecombe and Simons scored off the roll.

When the Pacers helped to the roller, that opened up the shooters in each corner.

James directed traffic on offense to ensure that the Sixers spaced the floor properly. Here, he waved Dean Wade out of the dunker spot and into the corner. Edgecombe promptly hit him for an open three off the short roll.

After all of the Sixers' success, the Pacers eventually switched the ball screen. That left the 6-foot-4, 173-pound Quenton Jackson on James, who has five inches and 77 pounds on him.

Jackson and James had already gone head to head on Sunday.

In the first quarter, James set a ball screen for Edgecombe which the Pacers switched. Edgecombe fed James in the post and the Pacers doubled him to support Jackson. James punished them for it.

They didn't double James after the switch in the fourth quarter. This time, James backed Jackson down, backed him down again and backed him down some more before scoring inside.

James' strength abusing matchups against smaller defenders after switches has been a safety valve for the Sixers' offense. Guards struggle to stop him from getting to the basket. When defenses send help, he picks them apart with his otherworldly passing ability.

That combination forced the Pacers to pick their poison. From the 9:55 mark in the fourth until the Pacers switched to a zone defense (after James bullied Jackson) with just over 6:00 left, the Sixers scored 13 points out of this action across seven possessions. That's a stellar 1.86 points per possession.

James, who's scoring a career-low 15.1 points per game so far this season, might not be the elite bucket-getter he was for most of his career. But he's still brilliant at conducting highly efficient offense that pressures a defense's pain points.

#2793. Mortgage demand turns lower, as interest rates shoot back up to the highest level in three weeks

Mortgage demand had been improving, as interest rates fell for about a month, but that came to an abrupt end last week. Mortgage rates moved sharply higher, causing total mortgage application volume to drop 2.3% from the previous week, according to the Mortgage Bankers Association's seasonally adjusted index.

The average contract interest rate for 30-year fixed-rate mortgages with conforming loan balances, $832,750 or less, increased last week to 7.35% from 7.21%, with points rising to 0.79 from 0.75, including the origination fee, for loans with a 20% down payment. That was its highest rate in three weeks.

"Treasury yields rose last week amid data showing inflation accelerated in October and Fed officials acknowledging that additional rate hikes may be needed in the coming months," said Joel Kan, vice president and deputy chief economist at the MBA.

Applications to refinance a home loan fell 2% for the week and were 54% lower than the same week one year ago. While overall refinance activity declined, Federal Housing Administration refinances increased almost 7%. The FHA offers low-down-payment government loans at lower interest rates but requires borrowers to pay mortgage insurance.

"The FHA rate was roughly 30 basis points below the conforming rate, which supported refinance opportunities for some homeowners," added Kan.

CNBC's Property Play with Diana Olick covers new and evolving opportunities for the real estate investor, delivered weekly to your inbox.

Applications for a mortgage to purchase a home fell 3% for the week and were 18% below where they were one year ago. Potential buyers are dealing with both affordability challenges as well as weaker confidence in the overall economy due to persistent inflation.

Mortgage rates haven't done much to start this week, staying essentially flat, according to a separate survey from Mortgage News Daily. There is more housing data set to be released this week with the Census report on monthly housing starts and building permits, as well as the monthly pending home sales index from the National Association of Realtors.

#2792. ‘Ridiculous’: Dale Earnhardt Jr. Shuts Down Chase Elliott Crew Chief Criticism

Dale Earnhardt Jr. defended the driver/crew chief pairing of Chase Elliott and Alan Gustafson in the wake of Hendrick Motorsports' 2027 lineup change. While HMS drivers William Byron and Alex Bowman are getting different crew chiefs, Elliott and Gustafson remain together for 2027.

On Monday, it was announced that Rudy Fugle will be atop the pit box for Bowman in 2027 after six seasons as Byron's crew chief. Adam Wall, who was the crew chief for Corey Day in the NASCAR O'Reilly Auto Parts Series, will move up to the Cup level as Byron's crew chief in 2027.

Meanwhile, the pairing of Elliott and Gustafson, as well as Kyle Larson and Cliff Daniels, will stay together next year.

Earnhardt Jr. discussed the shake-up at Hendrick Motorsports on the recent episode of "The Dale Jr. Download."

The former HMS driver said that some fans might look at the news of the crew chief changes and be angered by the fact Gustafson will continue to be Elliott's crew chief next year.

In the wake of the news, some fans have voiced their frustrations on social media under an X post by motorsports reporter Jeff Gluck regarding the matter.

The NASCAR Hall of Famer dismissed those voices, noting that the two enjoy working with each other. Earnhardt Jr. was blunt in saying the partnership is nobody else's business besides the people involved.

"There's this small little group of people that are seeing this news and going, 'They're going to leave Chase and Alan together still?' It's ridiculous. Chase and Alan want to work together. It's their [expletive] choice. None of y'all's business," Earnhardt Jr. said.

Earnhardt Jr. noted that Elliott has been on record saying he enjoys working with Gustafson and questioned why there is so much criticism around the crew chief.

"Chase likes it. He's said it multiple times. He likes Alan. What are we missing?"

Earnhardt Jr. acknowledges that fans are allowed to have an opinion and do not know the full behind-the-scenes story with things like this. However, because Elliott has expressed his desire to work with Gustafson,

Therefore, Earnhardt Jr. believes fans should give the pairing a break.

"This is something that Chase, multiple times, has said, 'I want to work with him. He's great.' Let's just leave it alone," Earnhardt Jr. said.

Perhaps some of the criticism regarding the Elliott/Gustafson combination stems from the performance of the No. 9 team in 2026.

Things started out promising for Elliott and company. The 2020 Cup champion won the seventh race of the campaign at Martinsville Speedway, the earliest in a season he had ever won before. Four races later, Elliott captured another victory at Texas Motor Speedway.

However, things went downhill from there as Elliott scored only one top-5 finish in the next 15 races. Elliott dropped from as high as third in the standings to eighth by the time the Chase began.

In the Chase, Elliott only led one lap throughout the 10-race period and fell back to 12th in the standings by season's end. Elliott scored 13 top-10 finishes, a career-low, and tied his career-low for average finish at 15.1.

Elliott only scored eight top-5 finishes during the 2026 season, the second-lowest in his career. His lowest was seven top fives in 2023, a year where he missed six races.

What started out as a strong year for Elliott turned into a difficult one as the season carried on.

#2791. Observations after Embiid posts season-high 37 points in Sixers' win over Nets

Observations after Embiid posts season-high 37 points in Sixers' win over Nets originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Joel Embiid's season-best 37 points led the Sixers to victory on Monday night in Brooklyn.

The Sixers notched a 135-123 win over the Nets to move to 12-3 on the season. Brooklyn fell to 3-10.

Jaylen Brown tied his season high with 31 points and set a new season high with 13 rebounds. Tyrese Maxey had 24 points and nine assists.

Julius Randle's 33 points topped the Nets.

On the second night of a back-to-back, the Sixers were without LeBron James (lower back/sciatica management), Dominick Barlow (lower back stress reaction), Adem Bona (cervical spine sprain) and Ariel Hukporti (right Achilles tendon tear). The Sixers are now 4-0 without James.

They'll visit the Magic on Wednesday night. Here are observations on the Sixers' win against the Nets:

Embiid returned after missing Sunday's victory against the Pacers with the "right foot plantar fascia injury management" injury designation that's become the norm for him early this season.

He had an especially aggressive start in New York and set plenty of middle ball screens for Maxey. When the Nets blitzed or hard hedged Maxey, Embiid found a nice soft spot around the foul line and wasn't hesitant to fire open jumpers. The 32-year-old big man posted 13 points in the first quarter on 6-for-11 shooting (1 for 2 from three-point range).

Beyond the stats, it must be encouraging for the Sixers to watch Embiid trust his instincts and jumper. We'll see whether he can sustain a good run and fully snap out of his shooting slump.

Maxey broke the scoring ice when he swiped a steal and then coasted in for a dunk. Dean Wade's put-back dunk gave the Sixers a 4-0 lead. Wade started in James' place for the fourth time and had eight points and 11 rebounds over 32 minutes.

With James out and Embiid back, Jabari Walker played at both power forward and center. He logged 16 minutes, a number that would've been higher without his foul trouble. Walker scored 12 points and grabbed five rebounds, the latest in a string of strong performances.

The Sixers extended their lead at the start of the second quarter.

Anfernee Simons drilled a corner three. The Sixers' three-man bench shot 6 for 9 from the floor in the first half.

Everyone who played for the Sixers chipped in something positive. Wade leapt up to snag a Walker miss and threw down an and-one dunk. VJ Edgecombe hit a floater to grow the Sixers' advantage to 50-30. Embiid had a well-worn path to the foul line in the second quarter and made 9 of his 10 free throws.

Sixers head coach Nick Nurse kept an eight-man rotation, only playing Simons, Walker and Kentavious Caldwell-Pope off the bench until the final 18.5 seconds.

The Sixers' collective effort on the glass was a major strength in the first half.

All of the starters besides Maxey recorded at least one offensive rebound. Brown had a double-double before halftime and poured in 19 points over the first two quarters.

The Sixers' focus and intensity slipped in the third quarter.

Nolan Traore's layup cut Brooklyn's deficit to single digits. Michael Porter Jr. hit a long two over Caldwell-Pope to trim the Sixers' lead to six points.

The Sixers concluded the third period on a high note. Walker sunk a buzzer-beating three to lift his team to a 105-95 edge. Again, the 24-year-old provided much more than intangible energy and made meaningful, momentum-shifting plays.

The action stopped with 9:02 left in the fourth quarter after Brown was fouled by Randle. The Sixers' star forward appeared to have his forehead collide with Randle's face. He was down for several minutes before going to the bench.

Randle needed stitches in his mouth before re-entering with 5:01 to go, Amy Fadool reported on the NBC Sports Philadelphia broadcast. Brown subbed back in with 6:02 remaining and a bandage on the cut above his left eye. However, he exited at the 1:44 mark and did not return.

Nurse told reporters in Brooklyn that Brown was out at the end of the game because of "tightness," according to PhillyVoice's Adam Aaronson.

After their shaky third quarter, the Sixers were ultimately fine in the fourth and reached a dozen wins.

Egor Demin's jumper pulled the Nets to within 125-121, but Embiid then iced the game by swishing a corner three.

#2790. U.S. soldier taken into military custody over suspected spying for Russia

LONDON — The American military has taken a U.S. Army soldier into custody after he was arrested in the U.K. on suspicion of spying for Russia, British authorities said Tuesday evening.

Senior serviceman Lt. Col. Brandon Carroll Tyner was arrested in England last week as part of an investigation by Britain's Counter Terrorism Policing with assistance from U.S. authorities, including the military and the FBI.

He was assigned to the NATO military alliance's Allied Rapid Reaction Corps (ARRC) and stationed at its headquarters in the county of Gloucestershire, working in human resources.

ARRC has more than 400 permanent staff from more than 20 nations ready to deploy land forces where they are needed.

The U.S. Army confirmed Tyner's identity to CNBC on Wednesday.

Following his arrest, the 41-year-old was detained in London on suspicion of committing offenses under Sections 1 and 3 of the U.K.'s National Security Act.

Authorities did not share details of the case, but Section 1 offenses relate to obtaining or disclosing protected information, while Section 3 covers assisting a foreign intelligence service.

CNBC reached out to the Russian government for comment on the latest developments in the case. Kremlin spokesman Dmitry Peskov told reporters on Nov. 11 that Moscow had "absolutely nothing to do with" the situation, following the soldier's arrest.

British prosecutors engaged with the U.K. Attorney General's Office and American partners after what U.K. officials called "an extensive investigation."

A decision was then made to pass the investigation to U.S. authorities, in line with international protocol.

FBI Director Kash Patel said in a statement on Tuesday that the service has "zero tolerance for anyone who betrays their oath to the United States or puts our national security at risk."

U.S. Brig. Gen. Sean Stinchon said in a statement that the case should serve as a "stark warning to any service member suspected of violating the oath we all take to protect and defend the Constitution."

"Whenever we suspect somebody of betraying the trust of their fellow soldiers and jeopardizing our collective defense, we will identify you, and we will ensure justice is served," he said.

"These are extremely serious allegations, and our collective response should send a strong message that we will act swiftly and robustly where we suspect this kind of espionage activity taking place," Laurence Taylor, head of the U.K.'s Counter Terrorism Policing, said as the updates on the case were announced.

"We will continue to support our U.S. colleagues as they take the matter forwards," he added.

Another NFL Sunday is in the books, with just one game left in Week 10 as the Baltimore Ravens host the LA Chargers on "Monday Night Football."

He led scoring drives on each of the Raiders' first two possessions and entered halftime with a 10-7 lead over the Seattle Seahawks, but Drew Lock rallied with two passing touchdowns in the third quarter to take the lead, improving to 3-0 as a starter for Seattle this season. Raiders star tight end Brock Bowers exited the loss with an ankle injury.

Follow along with Yahoo Sports for the latest NFL news, injury updates and more coming out of Week 10.

On the flip side, the Detroit Lions sat in the seventh slot just a month ago. They're now on a five-game winning streak and have zoomed up to the No. 2 seed.

The Tennessee Titans are adding wide receiver Wan'Dale Robinson to injured reserve due to an ankle injury, per team reporter Jim Wyatt.

Robinson suffered the ankle injury in the team's Week 10 loss to the Jacksonville Jaguars and could not return to the game. Restrepo was activated from the practice squad on game day and finished with 31 yards on three receptions.

Robinson has played in all nine games for Tennessee this season. He has brought in 38 receptions for 272 yards and a team-high three receiving touchdowns.

Head coach Robert Saleh has not revealed the extent of Robinson's ankle injury. If the wideout returns this season, he would first be eligible to be activated off IR in Week 15, ahead of the team's game against the Indianapolis Colts.

Tennessee will be without Robinson for at least the games against the Dallas Cowboys, Jacksonville Jaguars, Washington Commanders, and Detroit Lions.

Miami Dolphins defensive tackle Jordan Phillips suffered a torn biceps in last week's loss to the Indianapolis Colts that will sideline the 2025 fifth-round pick for the remainder of the season, according to ESPN's Marcel Louis-Jacques.

Phillips is the third Miami DT to sustain a season-ending injury in the past two weeks, per Louis-Jacques.

The Dolphins are 1-8 under first-year head coach Jeff Hafley. That said, their young core has flashed promise, especially on the defensive side of the ball.

The Commanders announced Tuesday that they are opening the 21-day practice window for running back Jacory Croskey-Merritt.

He has been sidelined due to a groin injury since Week 5. The running back remains on IR and must be activated before he can play in a game for Washington.

The New England Patriots and the Los Angeles Chargers were previously scheduled for that week's "Sunday Night Football" matchup.

With the injury to Patriots QB Drake Maye, and the Chargers' 1-8 start, the NFL has moved the game out of primetime.

The Cleveland Browns have been through this type of season before. It has just been a long time.

In 1979, the Browns had seven fourth-quarter drives to win games. In the 1980 season they were 11-5 and nine of the wins came by one score. That group got the nickname of the Kardiac Kids. Brian Sipe became a Cleveland legend, and won the 1980 NFL MVP award.

The 2026 Browns aren't at that level yet, but they're getting close. After rallying to beat the Houston Texans on a touchdown with 53 seconds left, the Browns are the first team in NFL history to win each of its first six games by four or fewer points, according to ESPN Insights. Every one of those wins has included a game-winning drive in the fourth quarter. It has been a magical, improbable run.

Detroit Lions head coach Dan Campbell won't let one freak injury change how he approaches the game. Following star pass rusher Aidan Hutchinson tearing his ACL during a celebration, Campbell said he will not discourage other Lions players from celebrating big plays moving forward.

Campbell, who made those comments during an appearance on Detroit's 97.1 The Ticket, explained that Hutchinson's injury was "just one of those things," and that's not enough for Campbell to establish a "nobody celebrates anymore" rule.

"I hate it, I know he hates it. But I mean, the number of moves that I've seen Hutch make and the number of plyometrics, explosive movements, the spins, the one-leg lateral movements, lateral balances, I'm just like, it was one of those things, man," Campbell said. "I hate it. I think we all understand what it is, and let's be smart, but no, I'm not gonna say, 'Nobody celebrates anymore.'"

When asked whether he felt he would need to address the issue with the team, Campbell shot down that idea. He then revealed that he once sustained an injury while celebrating a big play.

The Vikings and Raiders' playoff hopes are trending in different directions.

Minnesota's chances of advancing to the postseason improved to 90% with its Sunday win, per Next Gen Stats, even as it sits in second place in a crowded NFC North. The Raiders' chances fell to 10% after their loss, their 4-6 record paired with their remaining schedule projecting worse chances than also-currently-out-of-it AFC contenders in the New England Patriots, Baltimore Ravens and Houston Texans.

A similarity between the two teams whose fates will be tied this season by their shared Cousins custody: the expectation of evolution as the offensive cast settles into its roles.

There are quarterbacks like Josh Allen and Patrick Mahomes, who achieve stardom and become synonymous with one team. There are quarterbacks like … well, we don't need to name them, but quarterbacks who never capitalize on their draft-day promise and fade into the ether. And then there's Kirk Cousins, whose career has swooped and swerved in a way he never can on the field.

From spare-parts draft pick to decade-long starter, from chain-wearing goof to one of the highest-paid players in the history of the sport, Cousins' career is a study in improbability, and naturally, he's just landed in the unlikeliest position of all — on track for true postseason success. You like that?

#2785. Paris Hilton, Alexandria Ocasio-Cortez press House to pass Defiance Act to fight AI deepfake pornography

Artificial intelligence can turn an ordinary photo or video into a sexually explicit AI deepfake, with consequences that can follow victims into their classrooms and workplaces. Getting those images removed is one challenge. Holding the people who create or share them accountable is another. Policymakers, advocates and victims say additional federal legislation is needed, but a bill that would give victims new legal remedies has yet to receive a vote in the House after two years of efforts.

On Tuesday, Paris Hilton, one of the bill's most prominent advocates, was back on the steps of the U.S. Capitol alongside Defiance Act backers including Rep. Alexandria Ocasio-Cortez (D-NY) and Rep. Laurel Lee (R-FL) to press the U.S. House of Representatives to pass the bill into law. The U.S. Senate passed its most recent version of the measure by unanimous consent in January 2026.

The proposed bipartisan bill, the Defiance Act (Disrupt Explicit Forged Images And Non-Consensual Edits Act), would close gaps in an existing federal right to sue over sexually explicit deepfakes, creating the civil right to sue for victims of deepfake pornography. If passed, survivors could sue perpetrators who create, share, publish, solicit, or threaten to distribute this content without consent. Victims could also recover legal costs and seek court orders requiring the content to be removed.

"This work is very personal to me and it's just so terrifying now if AI gets into the wrong hands, what's possible," Hilton said during a new episode of the "CNBC Changemakers" podcast with Julia Boorstin.

When Hilton was 19, she was pressured into allowing an intimate recording featuring her and her then-boyfriend, Rick Salomon. She expected the recording to stay private and later found it was released without her consent.

AI has created an infinitely scalable platform for the online trauma she suffered.

"Somebody can literally just take an innocent photo and turn it into some explicit photo or video, and this is happening to millions of women and children all around the world, and they don't have anyone to protect them because the laws have just not caught up with this technology yet," Hilton said.

"There's girls who are teenagers in school. This is happening to them, and some of them are taking their own lives because it's just completely humiliated them, and destroyed their confidence ... being able to even walk into school," Hilton said.

Hilton is currently serving as chief advocacy officer at digital identity protection firm Loti AI, which helps people find unauthorized uses of their likeness or voice online and file takedown requests. She said the company has detected more than 100,000 explicit deepfakes depicting her and helped remove more than 60,000.

"I'm very fortunate that I have a team of lawyers. I have people helping me. But a lot of these young girls and women don't have that support," she said. "People need to know that there's going to be consequences for their actions," she added.

The Take It Down Act, which was signed by President Trump in May 2025, made certain disclosures of nonconsensual intimate images, including AI-generated deepfakes, a federal crime. Covered platforms must remove the material and any known identical copies within 48 hours of receiving a valid request.

Texas Sen. Ted Cruz introduced the bill in June 2024 after a case in Aledo, Texas, when an Instagram image of 14-year-old Elliston Berry was edited to make her appear nude and the altered image circulated on Snapchat. Berry told CNN that she woke up to calls and texts about the images. "It was out of my control. And they were spreading," she said. She also said it was difficult to tell her mother, even though she knew the images were fake.

As a mother, Hilton says she worries about what happens when these images circulate among classmates. "Even though the image is not real, the pain and suffering that comes along with it is very real," she said.

Before the Take It Down Act, state laws offered varying protections, and many did not explicitly cover synthetic images. That left victims with different options depending on where they lived, and, in some cases, reliant on platforms to act voluntarily. But Hilton says that current federal law sometimes "doesn't work," leaving victims in need of another way to hold perpetrators accountable. Other critics of the Take It Down Act's shortcomings have pointed to the reliance on federal prosecutors and lack of separate civil remedies for victims to pursue action.

The Sexual Violence Prevention Association, which helped author the Defiance Act, delivered letters to House Speaker Mike Johnson and House Majority Leader Steve Scalise on Tuesday, urging them to bring the bill to the House floor.

"I wrote the Defiance Act because survivors came to me with nowhere to go. Then, I was deepfaked by someone attempting to silence my advocacy for the Defiance Act. I was deepfaked while fighting for the very bill that would prevent it. Suddenly, I too had nowhere to go," said Omny Miranda Martone, founder and CEO of the SVPA, in a statement on Tuesday. "Now it's time for House leadership to say yes."

"The Defiance Act was designed to close those gaps. Once passed, it will help prevent digital sexual violence by giving survivors a way to hold perpetrators accountable and by ending the lack of consequences that emboldens them," Martone stated.

According to the SVPA, the Defiance Act has received 117 endorsements, including Microsoft and Snap, along with advocacy organizations such as National Organization for Women, Center for Democracy and Technology, and Center for AI Policy. In June 2026, OpenAI became the first AI company to endorse it, with Chris Lehane, the company's chief global affairs officer, calling it "smart legislation," and pushing for the House to pass it.

The Senate has passed versions of the bill twice, in 2024 and again in January 2026. Sens. Dick Durbin, the now-deceased Sen. Lindsey Graham (R-SC), Sen. Amy Klobuchar (D-Minn.) and Sen. Josh Hawley (R-MO) were among the original backers. Rep. Alexandria Ocasio-Cortez introduced the bipartisan House companion in May 2025, when it was referred to the House Judiciary Committee. It has never received a vote in the House.

In September, House Speaker Mike Johnson told reporters the bill was "good policy" but said the House had yet to build consensus around it. Some Republicans were reportedly reluctant to advance the measure this year because passage would hand Ocasio-Cortez a legislative win ahead of the midterm elections, according to a Politico report from July. Specific objections from House members have not been publicly shared.

The bill currently has 70 House cosponsors, evenly split between 35 Democrats and 35 Republicans.

If the House doesn't act before the current Congress ends, supporters would have to reintroduce the bill in the next Congress. Its path in January will depend on the new makeup of the House and whether its Democratic leaders make the measure a priority. The Democrats are projected to hold a majority in the new Congress, though its exact size is not yet known.

Follow and listen to CNBC Changemakers: Women Leading in the Age of AI wherever you get your podcasts.

If you are having suicidal thoughts or are in distress, contact the Suicide & Crisis Lifeline at 988 for support and assistance from a trained counselor.
