#2784. ‘Hard to win': Simons, Walker carry Portland lessons into Sixers roles

'Hard to win': Simons, Walker carry Portland lessons into Sixers roles originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Anfernee Simons and Jabari Walker shared the floor for 128 games in Portland. The Trail Blazers won 43 of them.

They sat beside one another in the Sixers' interview room Thursday night after a victory and discussed what the other 85 taught them.

"Whether you've got a big role or a small role, it's hard to win in the NBA," Simons said. "We've seen it firsthand."

Their roles were considerably different then.

Simons was an established starter entrusted with creating and scoring. Walker, a 2022 second-round pick, was trying to establish himself in a rotation. During their three overlapping seasons, Portland went 33-49, 21-61 and 36-46. The Blazers missed the playoffs each year.

Both are now reserves on a Sixers team constructed to contend. In nine games they've both appeared in this season, Philadelphia is 7-2.

They provided 26 points, 11 rebounds and six three-pointers in the Sixers' 123-112 win over the Raptors at Xfinity Mobile Arena. Simons scored 17 points and shot 5 for 6 from three-point range. Walker recorded nine points and eight rebounds, including four on the offensive glass.

The Sixers outscored Toronto by 24 points during Simons' 23 minutes. Neither former Blazer committed a turnover.

Their production helped Philadelphia overcome a 17-point first-quarter deficit and improve to 10-3 without LeBron James.

"You learn once you come off the bench to kind of read the game before you get in there," Simons said. "Obviously, we started off kind of slow. You just want to go in, bring some energy, get some stops, get out and run. I think everybody who got off the bench brought energy."

Simons supplied an immediate lift with a deep three-pointer at the first-quarter buzzer. His shot ended an 8-0 Sixers run and trimmed Toronto's lead to nine.

He remained involved during an 11-0 burst late in the third quarter, knocking down a three and setting up Walker for a basket. Walker then made a three early in the fourth before Simons scored on the Sixers' next possession.

When Toronto pulled within six with less than five minutes remaining, Simons answered with his fifth three-pointer.

Making those shots is the most obvious way Simons can help a roster already featuring James, Joel Embiid, Tyrese Maxey and Jaylen Brown. He said the Sixers have not made him feel as though every opportunity must go in.

"They encourage us to keep shooting the ball," Simons said. "Knowing that a team has that much confidence in us to keep shooting makes the game that much easier. Whether you miss or make, as long as we get the right shots in the right moments, I think everybody can live with the misses and makes."

Simons spent seven seasons in Portland before playing last year with Boston and Chicago. His move to Philadelphia reunited him with Walker and Rayan Rupert, another former Blazer who joined the Sixers on a two-way contract during the offseason.

Knowing familiar faces awaited him helped Simons enter another new locker room.

"It made the transition seamless," he said. "Just being able to have some comfortability with guys that I played with for multiple years. It's been fun, kind of feeling like a little bit of old times. Me, Bari and Rups have been through a lot."

Walker's path to Philadelphia began a year earlier and required an additional step.

After playing 188 games over three seasons with Portland, Walker signed a two-way contract with the Sixers in July 2025. He earned a standard contract in February and has since pushed his way into the backup-center conversation despite standing 6-foot-7.

Walker believes some of the work he did on losing teams was easily missed.

"When I first got to the league, I was part of a rebuilding process," he said. "I just felt like some of the little things I did didn't always get noticed when you're losing. I told myself, if I ever got to be part of a winning organization, then I'd go out and expand my abilities."

Rebounding has made Walker difficult for Nick Nurse to overlook.

Walker credited his timing and strong hands to lessons passed down by his father, former NBA center Samaki Walker. Those instincts have become especially useful while the Sixers piece together their frontcourt behind Embiid.

"Rebounding is a skill I kind of honed in on," Walker said. "My dad was a good rebounder. He was known for his rebounding and his timing. I think it's just a gift that he transferred to me."

Walker has made it clear that he expected to earn a role this season.

"I don't know if I was delusional, but I always felt like I was going to play and I was going to be a part," he said. "I read something where they said it's a surprise, but if you were around me this summer, it wouldn't be a surprise. This is expected, and I just want to continue to get better."

The freedom Simons once had in Portland is no longer necessary. Walker does not need a high volume of touches, either. Both understand that playing beside the Sixers' stars requires shooting when open, moving the ball and handling the less glamorous work.

"You've got to find ways to impact the game multiple ways, even if you're not scoring," Simons said. "Whether it's cutting, making the right read and pass to Joel, being the right swing pass to VJ, boxing out on defense or just being in the right position."

Their opportunities will fluctuate. Simons may take nine shots one night and only a few the next. Walker's minutes will depend on matchups and the health of Philadelphia's frontcourt.

Winning has changed what they are being asked to do. Their three years together in Portland taught them why the adjustment is worthwhile.

"You feel appreciative of the times where you go into a situation where your role might be a little bit less, but you're winning," Simons said. "That's something you can't take for granted."

#2783. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 dividend stocks for consistent income

Democrats' midterm election win has put the artificial intelligence trade in question, as voters clearly expressed opposition to the rapid buildout of data centers. The stock market could remain volatile amid AI trade concerns, macro uncertainties, and geopolitical tensions.

In this scenario, investors can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios to earn steady income. However, the extensive universe of dividend stocks can make selection challenging. Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as these experts assign ratings after in-depth financial analysis.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Viper Energy (VNOM) is a subsidiary of Diamondback and owns and acquires mineral and royalty interests, primarily in the Permian Basin. The company reported upbeat third-quarter earnings, supported by recent acquisitions and continued development activity from Diamondback and third-party operators. Viper announced a base cash dividend of 50 cents per share for Q3 2026, payable on Nov. 19. Based on the base and variable dividends announced over the past 12 months, VNOM's dividend yield stands at 5.7%.

Following the Q3 results, TD Cowen analyst Aaron Bilkoski reiterated a buy rating on Viper Energy stock with a price target of $61. The analyst noted that the company delivered solid Q3 results, with production, including oil and natural gas liquids, exceeding both TD Cowen and consensus estimates.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that the key update was the announcement of two royalty acquisitions for $809 million in cash, including deferred consideration, and $82 million of newly issued shares.

Given the recently announced base dividend hike, Bilkoski expects Viper to direct incremental free cash flow toward buybacks. While the acquisitions increase leverage, the analyst believes that Viper has enough capacity for opportunistic buybacks.

Bilkoski noted that Viper has historically delivered one of the highest production per share growth profiles in his royalty coverage, but still trades at a notable discount to the royalty peer average and the company's historical FCF yield range. "In our view, its above-average growth warrants a higher valuation," said the analyst.

Bilkoski ranks No. 525 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.80%. See Viper Energy Stock Buybacks on TipRanks.

Exxon Mobil (XOM) reported robust Q3 2026 profits, as its U.S. refineries boosted production to capitalize on the high diesel fuel prices sparked by the conflict in the Middle East. The oil and gas giant announced a quarterly dividend of $1.07 per share, payable on Dec. 10, reflecting a 3.9% increase. This marks Exxon's 44th consecutive year of dividend increases. At an annualized dividend of $4.28 per share, XOM offers a yield of 2.53%.

Impressed by the Q3 print, TD Cowen analyst Jason Gabelman reiterated a buy rating on Exxon stock and raised his price target to $193 from $180 to reflect higher downstream earnings, with the expectation of increased mid-cycle margins.

Gabelman noted that the strength in Exxon's refining business continues to offset Middle East-related headwinds. He added that the company increased its ownership in LNG projects, which should reduce its Middle East concentration over time from 75% to 50% and lower the likelihood of diversification-focused mergers and acquisitions. Beyond LNG, Gabelman expects Exxon's growth in the 2030s to come from Guyana, Kashagan, Permian, and possibly Venezuela.

Gabelman noted that Exxon announced a dividend hike of nearly 4% and plans to maintain buybacks at $20 billion for 2027. "The decision to maintain reduces the pro-cyclicality nature of the buyback," said the analyst.

Gabelman ranks No. 347 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.90%. See Exxon Mobil Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Williams (WMB), which owns and operates energy infrastructure to deliver natural gas, reported strong third-quarter results. The company announced a quarterly dividend of $0.525 per share, or $2.10 per share annualized, payable on Dec. 28. WMB offers a dividend yield of 3.09%.

In reaction to the Q3 2026 results, CIBC analyst Robert Catellier reaffirmed a buy rating on Williams stock with a price target of $82. The analyst noted that Q3 results were essentially in line with expectations and reflected continued strength in execution across WMB's infrastructure portfolio.

Catellier said that Williams' Q3 performance was driven by higher transmission, storage, and power generation revenues, as well as contributions from the Momentum Midstream acquisition.

He noted that Williams maintained its full-year EBITDA guidance of $8.3 billion to $8.5 billion. In fact, results are tracking toward the higher end of the guidance range, backed by contributions from recently completed projects and the Momentum Midstream deal. WMB continues to target long-term EBITDA compound annual growth rate of at least 11% through 2030.

Catellier expects WMB's long-term targets to be supported by incremental transmission and storage opportunities, including Transco's Southeast Supply Enhancement II project and an 84 MMcf/d expansion of the Garden Connector project.

Catellier ranks No. 667 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 9.60%. See Williams Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2782. Bayern Munich News: Arsenal wants Michael Olise; FC Barcelona eyeing Man City’s Josko Gvardiol?; and MORE!

Bayern Munich star Michael Olise will continue to be one of the most sought-after and speculated-upon players in the world until he signs a contract extension or announces his intention to transfer.

One club that is keeping a close eye on the Frenchman is Arsenal, which is looking to bring in a blockbuster-type player:

One Bundesliga superstar Arsenal are keeping tabs on is Michael Olise – despite not being a left-winger. More precisely, the Gunners are attentive to his contract situation and his impending negotiations with Bayern.

The Bavarians are desperate to hand the France international improved terms in order to guarantee that he will stay at the club beyond 2027. Olise has fielded interest from the likes of Real Madrid, PSG and Liverpool in recent months, hence the urgency.

We can confirm that a release clause will be one of the key points under discussion. Olise does not currently have one, but Bayern may have to compromise in order to get him to extend. The Munich outfit previously had a no-release-clause policy, but they have been forced to soften this stance in recent seasons, with both Harry Kane and Jamal Musiala – their best-paid players – having exit mechanisms in their contracts.

Given Arsenal's financial strength and willingness to pay whatever it takes for players of the calibre of Vini Jr and Olise, a release clause could make a transfer to North London possible even if he were to sign a new deal.

However, it must be emphasised that this is all hypothetical right now. Arsenal are not actively pursuing the 24-year-old at this stage; they are just keeping themselves informed on his situation.

Arsenal can join Real Madrid, Liverpool, Paris Saint-Germain, and the rest in line to take a run at Olise. Bayern Munich will not flinch until the summer, when extensive talks are expected to take place. In this case, it at least appears that everyone understands Olise will not be leaving midseason. If he decides that he wants to leave Bayern Munich, that is where it will all get interesting. With a contract that runs through 2029, the Bavarians would have some decisions to make at that point.

However, there is also the possibility that he signs a contract extension—a proposition that most Bayern Munich fans would prefer.

FC Barcelona is reportedly hungry to bolster its backline. With Manchester City's future uncertain, the Catalans could be looking to convince Joško Gvardiol to make the jump to LaLiga:

Manchester City defender Josko Gvardiol is on the radar of Barcelona, according to AS. Gvardiol, 24, has become one of the best young defenders in Europe in recent years and Barca are keen to strengthen their defensive options. However, while the club are interested in signing the Croatia international, they do not think a deal will be possible unless he pushes to leave amid the fallout from City's financial charges.

#2781. Democrat Mary Peltola wins Alaska Senate election

Democratic candidate Mary Peltola won the Alaska Senate election, capping a midterm cycle that already was one of the best for Democrats in more than a half-century, the Associated Press reported Tuesday evening.

Peltola defeated incumbent Republican Sen. Dan Sullivan in a race that was surrounded by controversy over an independent candidate who shared his name.

Peltola had faced accusations that her campaign recruited ultra-longshot candidate Dan J. Sullivan to the race to confuse voters. The Justice Department filed a lawsuit seeking to delay certification of the election if the incumbent Sullivan lost in order to conduct an investigation into whether voter confusion tainted the outcome.

The Justice Department is appealing the dismissal of that civil suit. The department is also conducting a criminal investigation into the second Sullivan's decision to enter the race.

Peltola's victory gives Democrats a solid majority of 56 seats in the Senate beginning in January.

Democrats this cycle flipped nine Senate seats that are currently held by Republicans.

That outcome, which was not considered remotely likely at the beginning of the election, came as part of a massive Democratic wave this year, which saw the party also flip majority control of the House of Representatives by a decisive margin.

Alaska was the last Senate race to be called in the midterm elections. Peltola, a former House member, joins Senator-elect Peggy Flanagan as the first two Native American women elected to the Senate.

The race was called two weeks after Election Day, and six days after Peltola's fellow Democrat Scott Colom pulled off perhaps the most stunning upset of the 2026 cycle by defeating incumbent Republican Sen. Cindy Hyde-Smith in the Mississippi Senate race.

As of 10:39 p.m. ET, Peltola had 50.1% of the vote, and Sullivan had 45.8%. Another Republican, Gerald Heikes, had 2.1% of the vote, while Daniel J. Sullivan had 1.6% of the vote.

If no candidate exceeded 50% of the vote, the election would have been decided through a ranked-choice method, in which the two bottom finishers would have been eliminated, and the second choices on the ballots of voters who selected them would be allocated to the remaining two candidates.

"From Juneau to Utqiagvik, the message from Alaskans is clear: the status quo in DC is not working for Alaska," Peltola said in a statement.

"Over the course of this campaign I've spoken with elders in tears about empty fish racks; families struggling to find housing they can afford; and parents in the Bush faced with the impossible choice of heating their homes or putting food on the table," Peltola said. "Today we take a monumental step forward in addressing those issues and putting Alaska first."As a lifelong Alaskan who has dedicated my life to serving our state, there is no greater honor than having Alaskans' support in our fight to take on corporate greed, lower costs, and put Alaska first," she said. "Thank you Alaska. Let's get to work."

#2780. Observations after Brown-Maxey duo scores 58 points in Sixers' win without Embiid

Observations after Brown-Maxey duo scores 58 points in Sixers' win without Embiid originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Jaylen Brown-Tyrese Maxey duo starred, LeBron James reached another milestone, and the Sixers earned a win without Joel Embiid on Sunday afternoon.

They improved to 11-3 on the season with a 131-124 victory over the Pacers at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

Brown scored 31 points and Maxey posted 27.

LeBron James had 15 points and 11 assists. He passed Jason Kidd on Sunday for third in NBA history with 12,092 assists.

Pascal Siakam scored 31 points for the 5-9 Pacers.

On the first day of a back-to-back, the Sixers were down Embiid (right foot plantar fascia injury management), Dominick Barlow (lower back stress reaction), Adem Bona (cervical spine sprain) and Ariel Hukporti (right Achilles tendon tear). The Pacers' injured players included Tyrese Haliburton and Andrew Nembhard.

The Sixers will face the Nets on Monday night in Brooklyn. Here are observations on their win over the Pacers:

Jabari Walker started for the first time this season, opening the game at center. He's been in the Sixers' rotation the past five games since Bona's injury.

Walker set up the Sixers' first basket, swiping a steal and then tossing the ball ahead to Maxey for a fast-break dunk. Maxey and Brown both began well and combined to score all of the Sixers' first 15 points.

Though he conceded five inches to 7-foot Pacers center Ivica Zubac, Walker was solid in his first stint. He got an early post stop on Zubac and then snatched a strong defensive rebound.

Dean Wade was next up for the Sixers at the five. He sunk a three-pointer on his first shot to give the team a 20-16 lead.

Walker ended up playing 25 minutes and posting 11 points and seven rebounds. Going back to the preseason, his stock has steadily risen.

"Here we are again after another game saying Jabari played very well," Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said. "He plays hard, he rebounds, he's got another level of confidence with his shooting this year.

"It's kind of wash, rinse and repeat again with him (today). He was great. … Jabari went in there and battled (Zubac). I thought he certainly matched him, if not outplayed him, which is a heck of a job."

Wade had 12 points and seven boards in 35 minutes. Overall, the Sixers' lack of healthy conventional centers is still a clear deficiency. Of course, everything should be simpler once Embiid is back in action.

The Sixers' defense did not have a sparkling start. The Pacers tallied 35 points in the first quarter and shot 62.5 percent from the field.

Brown helped the Sixers avoid a substantial deficit by draining all sorts of jumpers. He rarely appears bothered by close contests. Brown moved to 7 for 9 from the floor when he knocked down a three early in the second quarter. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope's triple knotted the contest up at 37-all.

Nurse needed to stray from his Plan A rotation when VJ Edgecombe picked up his third foul with 9:56 left in the second quarter. Labaron Philon Jr., who got a game with the G League's Delaware Blue Coats on Friday night, subbed in.

For a rookie guard, Philon has looked comfortable and confident on offense in most of his NBA appearances. He swished a long-range jumper in the second quarter and saw a floater rim out.

Brown reached the 20-point mark and put the Sixers up one with a slam assisted by James late in the second quarter.

He ran into serious foul trouble in the third quarter. Brown was whistled for his fourth foul with 9:53 left in the period for an off-arm shove on Aaron Nesmith. Wade entered and Brown exited.

The Sixers' slim lead didn't slip away. Walker nailed threes on consecutive possessions and Maxey jammed in a dunk.

Soon after, Tacko Fall came in for his first meaningful minutes as a Sixer. The 7-foot-6 two-way contract player grabbed an offensive board off a missed Edgecombe free throw, which drew one of the day's loudest cheers. With Fall on the floor, Maxey and James connected on an alley-oop that extended the Sixers' lead to 82-73.

When the Sixers tried to sit in a zone, Fall was called for a defensive three-second violation. Whatever the scheme, most of Fall's defensive work will come around the paint. He certainly has exceptional physical traits as a rim protector and is not nimble on the perimeter.

Fall logged five scoreless third-quarter minutes and recorded three rebounds. The Sixers outscored Indiana by a point with Fall on the court. Given Fall is occupying a (two-way) roster slot and the Sixers are so depleted at center, it made sense to see a bit of what he can do outside of garbage time. While five minutes isn't enough for any grand conclusions, it's very safe to say Fall is a unique player.

"I'm 7-6, so I can change the game in so many ways," Fall said. "Offensively, setting screens, making sure guys get open, being an easy target for lobs. Defensively, I feel like I've always been an elite rim protector and I've worked on my body a lot to be able to move a lot better."

Nurse praised Fall's outing, saying he "gave us some great minutes and a nice lift."

Fall is appreciative of his first NBA opportunity since the 2021-22 season.

"I've worked very hard," he said. "God has brought me back here and I'm thankful. And I'm ready. Whenever my name is called, I'm going to go out there and do my job."

The Sixers built their advantage to 102-87 with a Brown three early in the fourth quarter. They couldn't cruise to a blowout, though.

Siakam kept hurting the Sixers as a driver and had a highly efficient fourth quarter, scoring 19 points on 7-for-9 shooting. Quenton Jackson's layup trimmed Indiana's deficit to 119-115.

The Sixers ultimately held off the Pacers' push.

Caldwell-Pope canned a three, Wade threw down an and-one dunk, and James checked off his milestone assist with a late dish to Edgecombe for a layup.

#2776. NFL flexes Raiders-Browns to NBC's Sunday Night Football, Patriots-Chargers out

NFL flexes Raiders-Browns to NBC's Sunday Night Football, Patriots-Chargers out originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The league on Tuesday flexed the Las Vegas Raiders-Cleveland Browns Week 12 matchup into NBC's Sunday Night Football slot. It was initially slated for a 1 p.m. ET start.

The New England Patriots–Los Angeles Chargers game that was initially set for primetime will move up to a 4:05 p.m. ET kickoff and will be on CBS.

The NFL can flex games up to 12 days before they are scheduled, though Week 12 caused rarer challenges due to Thanksgiving week featuring more primetime games than usual.

Despite making the Super Bowl last season, the Patriots have regressed after a 5-5 start. Quarterback Drake Maye is also now set for an extended spell on the sidelines, causing further issues for an ailing team.

The Chargers have been one of the surprises of the season, but for all the wrong reasons. Justin Herbert and Co. went winless for their first eight games. They just finally took down a Jacoby Brissett-led Baltimore team to get to 1-8.

Las Vegas will enter the matchup with No. 1 overall pick Fernando Mendoza taking over from Kirk Cousins, who was traded to Minnesota on deadline day. The 4-6 Raiders will be on primetime for the first time this season. Their last SNF game was in 2023.

Cleveland is 6-4 and currently occupies the final AFC playoff spot as Deshaun Watson returns to form. The Browns are 4-0 at home for the first time since 1963, though they've eked out several close wins. Cleveland last hosted an SNF game in 2019.

Its most recent SNF game was in 2021 at Baltimore, and its only other primetime game this season was a Thursday night Week 4 home win vs. Pittsburgh.

The league also announced the Tennessee Titans-Jacksonville Jaguars game will move up to 1 p.m. ET.

#2775. Inside Sixers-Nets: How Philly can finish a back-to-back sweep in Brooklyn

Inside Sixers-Nets: How Philly can finish a back-to-back sweep in Brooklyn originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers will arrive in Brooklyn with one victory already secured on their back-to-back and a different combination of stars likely available for the second.

Philadelphia improved to 11-3 with a 131-124 win over the Pacers on Sunday. Jaylen Brown scored a season-high 31 points, Tyrese Maxey added 27 and LeBron James recorded 11 assists to move past Jason Kidd for third in NBA history.

James has not played both ends of a back-to-back this season, making it reasonable to expect him to sit Monday night. Joel Embiid, meanwhile, rested Sunday and is expected to return against Brooklyn. The Sixers had not submitted their official injury report as of Monday morning.

The Nets enter at 3-9, but this is not the same roster that finished 20-62 last season. Before the Sixers attempt to win twice in two nights, here is a closer look at what has changed in Brooklyn and where Monday's matchup could turn.

Nic Claxton is now in Chicago. Julius Randle arrived from Minnesota as the central piece of a four-team trade. Keon Ellis and Moe Wagner joined in free agency, while Michael Porter Jr. remains one of Brooklyn's primary scoring options.

Porter is available after missing Saturday's game for personal reasons. He will give the Nets another shooter after Brooklyn struggled badly without him in a 129-110 loss to Memphis.

The Nets' rotation also includes a large group of young players. Egor Dëmin, Nolan Traore and Mikel Brown Jr. have all received minutes in the backcourt, while Noah Clowney, Danny Wolf and Day'Ron Sharpe have played in the frontcourt.

The combination has produced uneven results. Brooklyn averages 110.2 points and allows 116.4 per game. Its minus-6.3 net rating ranks 23rd in the league.

Brooklyn has shown its offensive ceiling. The Nets scored 145 points in a 34-point win over New Orleans last week. Three nights later, they fell behind Memphis by 26 and lost by 19.

Jordi Fernandez has been direct about the difference between the team he sees in practice and the one that has appeared in games.

"We practice better than we play," the Nets head coach said after Saturday's loss. "It's a frustrating sign because you believe you're better."

The Nets did not merely miss shots against Memphis. Their problems began with their approach.

"We didn't punch first," Randle said. "Just organization. I think maybe just trying too hard, playing a little unorganized, stuff like that. And then just attention to detail. I think we've got a lapse of focus sometimes."

Fernandez was even more direct when discussing his young guards.

"They've got to grow fast," he said. "Being 20 is not an excuse."

That inexperience has appeared in Brooklyn's ball control. The Nets' offensive turnover rate ranks 28th in the league.

They have been much more disruptive on the other end. Brooklyn has forced turnovers on 13.9 percent of its opponents' possessions, tied for seventh.

The Sixers forced 10 Indiana turnovers and converted them into 19 points Sunday. Philadelphia committed 13 total turnovers while playing at the quick tempo it has emphasized since training camp.

Brooklyn has enough length and athleticism to disrupt the Sixers. It has not consistently maintained its own organization after creating that pressure.

Brooklyn has allowed 14.2 three-pointers per game, the eighth-most in the NBA. Opponents have posted a 55.2 effective field-goal percentage against the Nets.

That presents another opportunity for a Sixers team receiving strong outside shooting from several role players.

Anfernee Simons ranks third in the league with 33 three-pointers off the bench and is shooting 41.8 percent from deep. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope has made 46.5 percent of his threes, while Dean Wade is at 41.4 percent.

Maxey made five threes against Indiana and Brown added three. Jabari Walker also continued his strong start by making three of his six attempts from deep.

Those contributions matter on the second night of a back-to-back, especially if James sits. Philadelphia should not need Maxey and Brown to create every possession from a standstill if its shooters continue punishing open space.

Embiid's expected return would change the shape of Philadelphia's offense after James served as its primary organizer Sunday.

Brooklyn no longer has Claxton to use against Embiid. Sharpe has moved into a larger role and is coming off 16 points and 11 rebounds in Memphis. Wagner, Clowney and Randle give Fernandez several other bodies to try, but none presents the same defensive profile as Claxton.

Sharpe's work on the glass deserves attention. The Sixers have survived significant rebounding deficits this season, but allowing Brooklyn extra possessions would give Porter and Randle more chances to settle into the game.

On the other end, Embiid should have opportunities to force Brooklyn's frontcourt into difficult decisions. Sending help would create openings for Philadelphia's shooters. Defending him individually would leave Sharpe, Wagner and Clowney with a demanding assignment.

The Sixers have already won three games without James. Monday could require them to do it again, this time with Embiid returning as the center of the offense.

Brooklyn's record suggests a favorable matchup. Its shooting and young talent make it less comfortable than the standings indicate.

The Sixers handled the first half of their back-to-back by producing a season-high 131 points. Finishing the sweep will require them to adjust to a different available roster while preventing an inconsistent Nets team from finding one of its explosive nights.

#2774. Jensen Huang has agreed to appear before Senate banking committee, Chairman Scott says

Former Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang has agreed to appear before the Senate Banking Committee, panel Chair Tim Scott, R-S.C., said on CNBC's "Squawk Box" on Wednesday.

Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick has also agreed to testify, Scott said. The agreements to appear voluntarily come as pressure builds on Capitol Hill to investigate Huang's affair with a suspected Chinese spy, which led to his ouster at the chipmaker he founded.

"They both have agreed," Scott said. "We will hopefully have this done before Christmas."

Huang reached an agreement on Tuesday with the chairmen of three House committees for a closed-door transcribed interview on Dec. 1 jointly before the three panels. Scott had asked Huang to appear publicly in the Senate.

Scott said the appearances will provide the committee with "more information about what they knew, when they knew it and why they didn't do something about it."

The outgoing banking committee chair also homed in on Lutnick, calling for answers as to why the commerce secretary did not act after being warned about Megaspeed — the Singaporean company where the alleged Chinese spy, Alice Huang, was an executive. Nvidia sold its chips to Megaspeed, which U.S. investigators suspect of diverting them to China.

"They were warned as early as March of this year, several months ago, that Megaspeed should be on the 'entity list,'" Scott said, referring to a Commerce Department list of companies the U.S. views as national security risks. The Commerce Department's Bureau of Industry and Security "didn't do it, Howard didn't do it, we want to know why."

Lutnick and Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent have been embroiled in an intra-administration battle as the fallout from the Huang affair grows. Bessent on CNBC on Friday appeared to blame Lutnick for a lack of action on Megaspeed.

"You will have to ask them about their internal processes," Bessent said, referring to Commerce and noting his department has long supported imposing restrictions on Megaspeed.

A spokesperson for Huang declined to comment on Scott's announcement that he has agreed to appear.

Huang's upcoming appearances are likely just the start of the embattled former CEO's problems on Capitol Hill. Democrats will take control of Congress in January and have demanded sweeping investigations into the affair and Huang's cozy relationship with President Donald Trump.

#2772. Updating Eagles' 2027 draft pick after Week 10 bye week

Updating Eagles' 2027 draft pick after Week 10 bye week originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Eagles (2-7) had their bye week in Week 10 but didn't get much help.

They are still sitting in the No. 5 draft slot in 2027.

The Chargers won their first game of the season on Monday night but still have just one win. The Colts were able to able to beat the Dolphins but the Jets lost their eighth game. So the Colts pick and the Jets picks swapped but there are still four teams ahead of the Eagles.

After the Chargers won a game, the Eagles now have the longest losing streak in the NFL at seven games.

As a reminder, the Eagles did pick up an additional first-round pick from the Jaguars in a trade for Jordan Davis. But that will likely be toward the end of the first round.

Here's the remaining strength of schedule for those teams in the top 10 right now. The Eagles have the third most difficult schedule among them:

1. Titans: .5002. Chargers: .4933. Dolphins: .5874. Jets: .4925. Eagles: .5076. Colts (Jets): .4927. Cardinals: .4548. Falcons: .5239. Texans: .43310. Buccaneers: .413

The Eagles have drafted in the top five just six times since 1970: DE Richard Harris (No. 5 in 1971), OT Jerry Sisemore (No. 3 in 1984), WR Kenny Jackson (No. 4 in 1984), QB Donovan McNabb (No. 2 in 1999), OT Lane Johnson (No. 4 in 2013), QB Carson Wentz (No. 2 in 2016).

Since becoming Eagles GM in 2010, Howie Roseman has made just four picks in the top 10: Lane Johnson (No. 4 in 2013), Carson Wentz (No. 2 in 2016), DeVonta Smith (No. 10 in 2021) and Jalen Carter (No. 9 in 2023).

The Eagles during their bye week did pick up an additional first-round pick in a trade for Jordan Davis. The Jaguars are 7-2 and atop the AFC South so that pick will likely be in the 20s or worse.

In addition to those two first-round picks in 2027, the Eagles also have a second-round pick, a projected third-round compensatory pick, a fourth-round pick, three fifth-round picks and a seventh-round pick.

#2771. Tokenization platform Securitize gains 10% postmarket on Q3 results, move into asset-backed finance

Tokenization platform Securitize climbed about 10% postmarket after reporting third quarter growth alongside increased operating expenses, and announcing an expansion into asset-backed finance through a strategic acquisition.

Miami-based Securitize, which made its public market debut in July, operates in the relatively new corner of the crypto equity universe involved in institutional tokenization infrastructure. Tokenized assets on the platform grew 9% in the third quarter, to $4.8 billion, from the same period a year ago. Aggregate transaction volume rose 20% from a year ago, to $4.3 billion. Revenue of $11.5 million was up 28% from last year.

The growth came as bullish sentiment around tokenization gained traction throughout the third quarter, ahead of the Security and Exchange Commission formalizing the long awaited Innovation Exemption on Sept. 17, which created a temporary pathway for trading of certain tokenized stocks and strengthened the regulatory outlook.

Profitability is still meaningfully below year-ago levels, due in part to costs associated with going public over the summer, as well as the scaling up of the tokenization business at an active time for the entire sector, Securitize CEO Carlos Domingo told CNBC in an exclusive interview.

Securitize reinforced "certain areas of the company like finance, compliance, legal [and] cybersecurity," which increased corporate costs "compared to a year ago when we were privately held," Domingo said. "We're in in a very good position. The adjusted EBITDA … is improving significantly from previous quarters."

Securitize reported an adjusted EBITDA loss of $2 million, compared with positive adjusted EBITDA of $3.6 million a year ago, better than a $5.5 million loss in the second quarter. The company posted a 183% increase in operating costs and expenses.

Also on Monday, the Securitize board authorized a $50 million share buyback and announced the acquisition of River One, an institutional asset-backed financing platform developing technology to connect stablecoin capital with traditional lending markets. Terms were not disclosed.

Securitize sees an opportunity to modernize the $9 trillion market for asset backed finance by using stablecoins to finance real world assets, generate yield and tokenize investment positions and credit products, Domingo said.The acquisition also marks an expansion beyond Securitize's core niche markets at a time when tokenization's competitive landscape is growing, with traditional asset managers like BlackRock and Franklin Templeton, and established financial market infrastructure providers like DTCC and Broadridge, entering the industry.

"We have been very active in tokenized funds, treasuries [and] yield … And we've been obviously recently very active in tokenized stocks, so this adds another piece of the puzzle in the emerging tokenization space for us to capture revenue growth."

#2769. How to get Anthropic stock — without buying the IPO

Anthropic's initial public offering on Thursday may be the largest in history — and everyday investors can get a piece of the stock without buying the IPO directly.

Many actively managed mutual funds and exchange-traded funds already own pre-IPO stock in the artificial intelligence startup — and investors in those funds will automatically get a sliver of Anthropic's IPO when it starts trading. Investors in certain index funds will gain access to the stock within weeks, though timing will vary by fund.

Investors' exposure to Anthropic in mutual funds and ETFs will likely be relatively low — well below 1% of the overall portfolio for most funds, experts said. That dynamic helps investors avoid being too concentrated in any one stock, thereby reducing risk and diluting the volatility that generally accompanies an IPO.

"I would certainly not recommend to anybody putting all of your eggs in one basket with any stock," said Jay Ritter, director of The IPO Initiative at the University of Florida.

It's an important point, as the Anthropic IPO could be the biggest ever.

The company is expected to raise up to roughly $90 billion in its market debut — a sum that would beat current record holder SpaceX, which raised $86 billion in a blockbuster IPO in June.

The IPO comes at a time when some market watchers are questioning if Jensen Huang's recent resignation as CEO of chipmaker Nvidia — following the revelation of his extramarital affairs — threatens to derail the AI boom.

Anthropic won't be available in index funds — which track the returns of a specific market index like the S&P 500 — right when trading starts.

Each market index has different rules that govern when specific stocks become eligible for inclusion.

Some popular indexes will add Anthropic relatively quickly, Ritter said.

One example is the Morningstar U.S. Total Market Index, he said, which will add Anthropic stock after a five-day waiting period following its IPO.

Funds that track this index — such as the $1.8 trillion Vanguard Morningstar Total Stock Market Index Fund (VTI), one of the largest stock ETFs — will likely add Anthropic stock by the end of November, said Brendan McCann, senior associate manager research analyst at Morningstar.

MSCI, another index provider, adds large IPOs after 10 trading days.

Funds that track its indexes — including the iShares MSCI USA ETF (MLRG), which tracks the returns of large- and mid-cap U.S. stocks — should add Anthropic in early December, McCann said.

Index providers like Nasdaq and FTSE Russell earlier this year accelerated their timelines to add large IPOs, to accommodate interest in SpaceX's market debut.

However, in both cases, investors will likely have to wait longer to access Anthropic stock than they did to get SpaceX, experts said

FTSE Russell indexes like the Russell 3000 — which tracks the performance of the 3,000 largest stocks trading on U.S. exchanges — typically have a waiting period of five days to add large IPOs. The iShares Russell 3000 ETF (IWV) and Vanguard Russell 3000 ETF (VTHR) track this index, for example.

However, Anthropic won't be available in FTSE Russell indexes until the market opens on Dec. 14, the provider said on Friday.

That's because the typical five-day window would fall close to Thanksgiving Day, FTSE Russell said. Instead, the provider will wait to add the stock until its semiannual review period in December.

The waiting period will be about three weeks, compared to about one week for SpaceX, McCann said.

Similarly, Nasdaq would typically have a 15-day waiting period to add Anthropic stock to its indexes, such as the Nasdaq-100. This index measures the performance of 100 of the largest non-financial companies listed on the Nasdaq stock exchange, many of them in the technology sector.

That waiting period would normally end on Dec. 11.

However, the Nasdaq-100 won't add Anthropic until after Dec. 18, when Nasdaq completes its periodic review of index constituents, McCann said. The delay is due to Nasdaq's explicit methodology for timing IPO additions.

In both cases, the delays are "nothing about [Anthropic] being inherently riskier than SpaceX," McCann said. "It's completely an IPO timing thing."

The $463 billion Invesco QQQ Trust (QQQ), one of the largest stock ETFs, is an example of a fund that tracks the Nasdaq-100, Ritter said.

Meanwhile, investors in the S&P 500 — perhaps the best-known stock index — will have to wait at least a year for Anthropic to join the ranks.

The provider, S&P Dow Jones, requires companies to be public for at least 12 months to be eligible for inclusion in the S&P 500. Additionally, the company must be profitable.

Anthropic's weighting in specific index funds depends on factors like how high the stock price goes and how the index calculates the "float."

The float is the total number of shares available for public investors to buy and sell, though Ritter said there isn't a uniform definition of which types of shares are included in this calculation.

Ritter estimated Anthropic shares would represent about 0.15% of the Morningstar U.S. Total Market Index. He made this projection on Monday based on various index characteristics and assumptions like a $1.5 trillion Anthropic valuation. Estimates suggest its valuation will be in that ballpark.

The Nasdaq-100 will have a larger weight of about 0.8%, Ritter calculated.

However, that's higher than most, experts said.

"Most [index funds] will only be picking up Anthropic in the neighborhood of 10-basis-point positions," said Stephen Welch, a senior equity strategy analyst at Morningstar. Ten basis points equals 0.1%.

Some actively managed funds have much larger positions, thanks to private Anthropic stock that asset managers purchased before the IPO.

Investors in these funds will have effectively bought the IPO as soon as trading starts.

For example, the Coatue Innovative Strategies Fund had a roughly 15.7% weighting to Anthropic as of June 30, according to the most recent Morningstar data.

This is a closed-end fund, which isn't as liquid as mutual funds or ETFs and generally isn't easily tradable, Welch said.

Everyday investors should largely stick to mutual funds or ETFs, he said.

The Tema Photonics & Optical ETF (LAZR) and Yorkville America 2X Long Mangos Plus Daily Target ETF (MNGZ) have the highest Anthropic weightings of any ETF or mutual fund — about 12.9% and 10.7% of overall fund holdings, respectively, as of early November, according to Morningstar.

However, long-term investors should likely avoid funds that have such concentrated holdings, Welch said.

"I don't think I'd recommend investing in something more specialized like that," he said. "It's a thematic, very niche ETF that won't be very diversified."

Ultimately, owning the "latest hot IPO" shouldn't be investors' primary reason for buying or selling a fund, Welch wrote in October.

"You should evaluate an equity fund on its manager, approach, parent company, and the role it plays in a long-term portfolio, not just on how well it's situated for the next record-breaking public offering," he wrote. "Chasing IPOs with funds can easily backfire."

#2768. Preview: Can the Utah Jazz finally get their second win vs. the LA Clippers?

There aren't many NBA franchises that have ever felt more hopeless than the Los Angeles Clippers in the aftermath of the Kawhi Leonard-Steve Balmer saga. They went all-in on the concept of Leonard alongside All-Star wing Paul George, mortgaging the house, the dog, and even undergoing construction of a state-of-the-art arena in which they could showcase their prosperous future. Instead, that core imploded. Leonard and George have both departed, and the Clippers were stripped of five first-round picks, with bans, suspensions, and fines abound.

It's a team without a future. Without hope for organic improvement. Without allure for potential free agent signings. So yeah, times are feeling a bit shallow in this shadowy corner of LA.

If anyone can relate to such a feeling of hopelessness, it's the Utah Jazz.

After four long years in the tanking pressure cooker, Utah's rebuilt product still came out half-baked. Losers of 12 straight, a veteran (expensive) frontcourt spending more time on the injury report than the basketball court, and good for at least one baffling implosion per game, Will Hardy's team is 1-13, tied for the worst record in the entire NBA, and light-years removed from the playoff hunt that had been promised pre-season.

Utah's young core has been underwhelming outside of Darryn Peterson, Keyonte George, and Ace Bailey. In all truth, Peterson's is the only name in that trio whose development has taken a clear and obvious upward trajectory. George's scoring efficiency and decision-making have fluctuated in quality, and his defensive impact remains minimal. Bailey's mastered the Warriors-era Shaun Livingston mid-range pull-up, but can't seem to hit his target from beyond the arc, shooting just over 26% on the young season.

Despite keeping the score tight against some of the best teams in the West, Utah still has no winning experience. When the game clock hits winning time, the Jazz's watches are unsynchronized.

The host Clippers, currently 4-8, are 6.5-point favorites at the time of this article. If the Jazz can't find a way to win this one, it's difficult to imagine where they might collect their second win of the season.

Calvin Barrett is the Associate Editor for SLC Dunk and a Utah Jazz columnist for the Standard-Examiner. Originally from Springville, Utah, he currently writes from his desk in Japan.

#2767. Trump arrives in China for historic meeting with Xi, Putin

President Donald Trump arrived in Shenzhen on Wednesday morning ahead of historic trilateral talks with Chinese leader Xi Jinping and Russia's Vladimir Putin.

Xi earlier welcomed President Donald Trump on the tarmac, which he said was a sign of reciprocity after Trump personally greeted him at the airport during a visit to the U.S. in September.

Trump is attending a welcome banquet alongside Secretary of State Marco Rubio, Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent and Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick.

Trump is in China for the APEC summit, which President Xi opened with calls for free trade and greater cooperation around artificial intelligence. Highly anticipated talks with Xi and Putin are set for Thursday.

Xi said Thursday's leaders' meeting would discuss "measures to expand mutual openness, explore paths for innovation-driven growth, and broaden practical cooperation in various fields," according to Chinese state media.The Chinese president told Trump he wanted to "advance a constructive, strategic and stable relationship between China and the US based on respect, fairness and reciprocity," according to Chinese state news agency Xinhua.Trump said he was confident the visit would advance bilateral relations and prove successful, Xinhua reported.

Xi said: "We should actively build partnerships for scientific and technological innovation, deepen cooperation in the digital economy and artificial intelligence and promote a green and low-carbon transition."

Putin has also addressed the summit, saying sanctions imposed on Russia have backfired and strengthened his nation.

The much-anticipated meeting between the three world leaders is set to take place on Thursday, with discussions expected to center on the Iran war and Russia's war in Ukraine.

Though expectations for any concrete outcome are low, Trump, Xi and Putin are expected to hold in-depth discussions on issues crucial for world peace and strategic stability, a spokesperson for China's foreign ministry said Monday.

North Korean leader Kim Jong-Un could attend the summit, which is a forum of 21 APEC member economies.

Trump flew in from Manila, Philippines, where he started a three-day visit to Asia and met key regional allies including Japan, India and Australia.

The APEC host city Shenzhen has been dubbed one of China's "Silicon Valleys" and has been a major hub for China's internet and artificial intelligence companies.

Beijing will position itself against what it calls "unilateralism and protectionism," with trade, artificial intelligence, and the Middle East expected to dominate the meeting's agenda.

Xi held several bilateral meetings with leaders on the sidelines Wednesday, including South Korean President Lee Jae Myung, with whom he discussed trade and commerce. Seoul's readout of the meeting also said the pair discussed peace on the Korean Peninsula, but this was omitted from Beijing's record of the talks.

The Chinese leader also met Canadian Prime Minister Mark Carney and Vietnamese President To Lam earlier in the week.

Xi met with Putin earlier Wednesday, before Trump's arrival. The Chinese president called for greater cooperation between the two countries and spoke of providing a more stable and predictable business environment, according to Chinese state media, while promoting a "multipolar world and democratization of international relations."

Putin said Russia was looking to expand cooperation in trade, energy and education. The reports did not address Russia's war in Ukraine or the Iran war.

Trump will also hold bilateral talks with Chinese President Xi Jinping, amid hopes the pair can secure a lasting trade truce beyond January 2027. The meeting comes as the U.S. seeks to curb exports of artificial intelligence chips and amid concerns that Nvidia chips may have been diverted to China.

#2766. Eagles add consultant who coached under Chip Kelly, Doug Pederson, Nick Sirianni

Eagles add consultant who coached under Chip Kelly, Doug Pederson, Nick Sirianni originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

T.J. Paganetti, who spent 10 seasons in two separate stints with the Eagles in a variety of roles and was part of both Super Bowl championship staffs, is returning to the club as a consultant, a league source confirmed following a report by ESPN's Adam Schefter.

Paganetti will serve in a similar role to previous consultant Jim Bob Cooter in 2021 and Marcus Brady in 2022 and 2023, advising Howie Roseman and Nick Sirianni on numerous topics.

"I don't comment on things like this often, but T.J. Is an outstanding coach," Jason Kelce tweeted last November after Paganetti was fired by the Saints. "He brought a ton of value for us in Philadelphia, and was a big part of our success up front.

"One of my favorite coaches from my time with the Eagles. He's extremely knowledgeable, driven, and always worked his ass off to put the players in positions to succeed, I respect him greatly.  A lot of teams are in need of good coaches, the team that hires him is getting a great one."

Paganetti, 37, began his coaching career in 2009 under Chip Kelly while he was still a student at the University of Oregon. When Kelly became Eagles head coach in 2013, he brought Paganatti with him as an offensive and quarterbacks analyst.

Paganetti returned to Oregon for two years in 2015 and 2016 and worked under new head coach Mark Helfrich, who Paganetti had worked with under Kelly. But Doug Pederson brought him back in 2017 as an offensive quality control coach and assistant offensive line coach under Jeff Stoutland. He remained in that role for two years.

In 2019, he became assistant running backs coach and in 2020 assistant run game coordinator.

Nick Sirianni kept Paganetti when he replaced Pederson and named him an offensive quality control coach in 2021. He became run game specialist and assistant tight ends coach in 2023 and then run game specialist and assistant offensive line coach in 2024.

Paganetti joined Kellen Moore's Saints staff as run game coordinator in 2025 after the two had worked together with the Eagles in 2024. But Paganetti was among a handful of assistant coaches fired halfway through the season with the Saints 3-6.

Paganetti's specific responsibilities are unclear, but the Eagles are an NFC-worst 2-7, have lost seven straight games and are buried in last place in the NFC East two years after winning the Super Bowl. So clearly management is looking for a fresh voice with varied NFL experience who can help turn the operation around.

Pagnetti's brother, Ryan, was with the Eagles from 2018 through 2020 as a defensive coach and is now a popular NFL analytics social media expert.

#2764. Celtics get good injury news ahead of game vs. Nuggets

DENVER — The Celtics will be at full strength when they face the Denver Nuggets on Wednesday night. Neemias Queta, who missed Monday's game to rest, is fully available.

Boston is coming off a 115-105 loss to the Orlando Magic, just its second loss in 13 games. The Celtics recovered from a sloppy first half to tie the game at 80, but Orlando scored 40 points in the fourth quarter to pull away.

"We fought our asses off to get it to a one-possession game," Joe Mazzulla said after the loss. "We take the lead, I think it's 80 to 80. There are three or four plays that we don't come up with that turn into catch-and-shoot threes or a layup for them. Those are the ones that you focus on."

Orlando did most of its damage inside, outscoring Boston 54-32 in the paint. Queta's presence was sorely missed, and Paul George said that the team did not defend as well as they should have.

"If anything, maybe we overrespected them behind the three-point line," George said. "We knew Orlando was a paint team. They like to drive and get to the rim. They got a lot of guys that kind of put their head down and get to the rim. So we kind of played into their strengths. So that's on us."

The loss came on the second night of a back-to-back, after the Celtics jumped out to a 42-9 first-quarter lead against the Dallas Mavericks on Sunday and ultimately won by 37.

Mazzulla said the team didn't overreact to the Mavericks win nor the Magic loss.

"We weren't jumping around for joy last night," he said. "I mean, again, you can't coach to the scoreboard."

"There's a lot of things we obviously can learn from, and going on the road tomorrow," Tatum said. "So obviously, probably watching film and regroup and just get ready for Denver."

The Nuggets are 7-5 and coming off a Monday night loss to the Phoenix Suns. They're been better on the road (5-2) than at home (2-3).

Jamal Murray leads the way with 26.1 points and 6 assists per game, while Nikola Jokic averages 24.1 points, 10.9 rebounds, and 8.9 assists.

#2763. Coreweave, Iren, Nebius shares sink following Nvidia earnings

Nvidia shares tried to rebound on Wednesday after the company issued its first earnings report since the departure of former CEO Jensen Huang.

Neoclouds bounced initially following the report before trading lower Wednesday. CoreWeave sank 6%, Iren about 3% and Nebius declined around 4%.

Nvidia's third-quarter revenue came in at $115.84 billion, more than $6 billion above analysts' expectations of $109.10 billion, while fourth-quarter guidance of $130 billion topped the $125.1 billion consensus, according to LSEG.

The semiconductor giant continues to navigate the fallout from Huang's resignation last week. Shortly before the results, Huang made his first public statement since resigning last week, saying that he was open to returning to the company, after an outpouring of support from prominent tech figures.

But Harvey Jones, the new and first-ever chair of Nvidia's board, said on the earnings call that the board does not intend to reinstate him as CEO.

″[Hyperscalers] are the ones that are right now feverishly looking for more focus in terms of the compute that we have available," interim CEO Colette Kress told Jim Cramer on CNBC's "Mad Money."

"This concept that anything is changing in terms of any of our types of customers — that is just not true. They are all continuing to be with us, and we have a very deep breath of different customers."

The U.S. Commerce Department announced a legal change on Monday to try and stem China's access to the most advanced Nvidia chips — which are already under export controls effectively preventing their import into the country — by restricting Chinese companies' ability to access compute remotely in overseas data centers.

Nvidia told investors in its earnings commentary that it was "not assuming any Data Center compute revenue from China in our outlook," in keeping with its recent language on the country.

— CNBC's Caleigh Keating contributed to this report.

#2762. International player Joshua Weru on his development behind the scenes

International player Joshua Weru on his development behind the scenes originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Joshua Weru was the talk of training camp this summer. While draft pick Uar Bernard grabbed headlines this spring, it was the Eagles' other International Player Pathway Program player who began to stand out in August.

The edge rusher from Kenya flashed in practices and even in the preseason a bit — to the point where some worried he'd be claimed if the Eagles dared to waive him to get him to the practice squad.

But that was months ago and now we're in November.

Weru has spent all season on the Eagles' practice squad. And most days this season, Weru walks through the Eagles locker room mostly unnoticed. He'll sit at his locker without being bothered and hadn't really been asked for an interview all season. Until last week. So Weru seemed a bit surprised when he was approached and asked for a few minutes, which he graciously handed over.

What have the last couple of months been like?

"It's been good," Weru said. "I continue to say I'm so lucky to be here. The amount of reps we get on scout team and developmental have been good. Just seeing things better and recognizing blocks quicker. Just getting my strike stronger. Even though I'm not necessarily getting stronger in the weight room, like actual game strength is getting slightly better. Just those things because you're more used to doing certain movements so you're getting better at it."

Weru, 23, hasn't been elevated yet this season so he hasn't played in a game since the preseason. Weru misses playing, but last week invoked the old proverb — absence makes the heart grow fonder — to explain his situation.

Over the last couple months, Weru knows he has gotten better. Although he's unsure how to quantify it, his growth is undeniable.

Away from the spotlight of training camp, Weru has settled into a rhythm as a practice squad player. Tuesdays are big for him to get in extra voluntary work. And then on days the Eagles practice, he goes out early to get in what Nick Sirianni always calls "500 shots" of extra work. Those extra sessions for Weru and fellow rookie Keyshawn James-Newby are run by outside linebackers coach Jeremiah Washburn and assistant linebackers coach Tyler Scudder.

But a big part of Weru's role as a special teams player is contributing on the scout team. So each week, he dons a pinny and tries to give the Eagles' offensive line the best looks he can for that upcoming game.

Jordan Mailata has seen a lot of Weru on the scout team this season and admitted the rookie got under his skin a little bit to end last week: "He's very fast and twitchy so he gave a great look this week."

Mailata admitted that because of Weru's lack of football knowledge, misdirections will fool him to where he'll over commit or lose contain in the run game. But Mailata has already seen Weru improve in those areas week by week.

"He's going through that but he's doing a great job of not taking that too personal," Mailata said. "He's hard on himself a good amount. His effort is greater than mine ever was. I just saw him chase down Saquon (Barkley) it was incredible. Incredible."

Weru takes pride in his job as a scout team player and keeps a close eye on the Eagles' offensive linemen on Sunday.

"That gets me fired up, getting to watch them," Weru said. "If I see that they've been doing well, I feel like I helped a bit at least."

The interesting part of being a scout team player for someone like Weru, who hasn't been playing football for very long, is that it exposes him to other styles of defense. This year on scout team, Weru has been learning how to play out of three-point and four-point stances, which has taken some getting used to.

Weru said he didn't realize how hard it would be to get out of a stance like that.

"I feel like I'm slowly getting better at that," he said. "It's also helped me get more reactive because it's not playing to the rules that we have. So you kind of have to know what's going on."

Weru has been practicing every week, while his fellow IPP teammate, Bernard, has been on the Practice Squad Injured List since Oct. 9.

Bernard and Weru were big stories back in training camp and many wondered if they would make the 53-man roster and feared if they didn't, another team would scoop them up. But both players were cut, cleared waivers and immediately signed to the Eagles' practice squad.

While Weru obviously wanted to make the Eagles' roster, he never wanted to leave. And now that he's away from the spotlight a bit, he has been able to grow each week behind the scenes.

No one really knows what the future holds for Weru. Even though the Eagles once hit it big on an IPP player in Mailata, the odds are stacked against Weru and Bernard. But there's no harm in trying and Weru has continued to put in the work.

"It's been really good because just growing and building relationships with guys in my room, coaches, the development staff, all my teammates," Weru said. "So I just feel increasingly comfortable. I can see how much time they're willing to invest in me. I'm just happy to be here."

#2761. Cantor Fitzgerald's Nvidia business interests targeted in Democrats' Lutnick probe

Democrats are scrutinizing the business of Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick's adult sons as they investigate why the top Trump administration official allegedly resisted cracking down on companies suspected of diverting Nvidia's advanced AI chips to China.

The probe unveiled Wednesday ratchets up the pressure Lutnick was already facing from inside and outside the Trump administration. It provides the latest sign that Nvidia, the scandal-scarred AI behemoth, will be embroiled in congressional Democrats' coming anti-corruption crusade, as well as a wider bipartisan effort to clamp down on AI chip sales to China.

Reps. Jamie Raskin, D-Md., and Gregory Meeks, D-N.Y., the respective ranking members of the House Judiciary and Foreign Affairs Committees, are demanding reams of records from Cantor Fitzgerald, the investment firm Brandon and Kyle Lutnick took over from their father after he joined President Donald Trump's Cabinet in February 2025.

"We seek to understand whether any actions or decisions by Secretary Lutnick concerning export controls, enforcement measures, or national security policies may have been improperly influenced by Cantor Fitzgerald's holdings or business interests," the Democrats wrote in a letter sent Tuesday to Brandon Lutnick.

At the time of Howard Lutnick's confirmation, Cantor had amassed a stake in Nvidia reportedly valued around $1.2 billion, one of its largest holdings for its clients. Between September 2025 and March 2026, the firm reported a nearly 50% decline in its Nvidia holdings, a CNBC analysis of securities filings found.

In the same time period, Lutnick had reportedly been pressed to take action against Megaspeed, a Singapore-based tech firm accused of transferring Nvidia AI chips to China. The New York Times reported in October 2025 that the Commerce Department was investigating Megaspeed's ties to Beijing.

Lutnick "repeatedly stood in the way" of implementing controls on Megaspeed and other companies suspected of funneling cutting-edge chips to China, Axios reported Nov. 11.

"Secretary Lutnick's reported obstruction may have resulted in billions of dollars of restricted AI chips being diverted to China," read the Democrats' letter, which was also signed by Rep. Sydney Kamlager-Dove, D-Calif., the ranking member of the Foreign Affairs Subcommittee on South and Central Asia.

Lutnick's reported dithering may also have propped up Nvidia share prices for months, "preventing devastating losses for your company," the Democrats wrote.

"If these media reports are accurate, the American people deserve to know: Did the Secretary of Commerce seriously and perhaps irreversibly compromise U.S. national security interests to make money for his adult children and Cantor Fitzgerald?" they asked.

There is no known evidence that Lutnick's sons had advance or insider knowledge or information about Nvidia or the investigations surrounding it.

Cantor, in a statement to CNBC, called the House Democrats' letter "inaccurate" and said any allegations that its work has influenced government decisions are "baseless."

"Cantor holds no material proprietary position in Nvidia. Cantor's Nvidia holdings arise primarily from equity swaps executed for clients. We buy the underlying shares to hedge those trades. The decline in our holdings since early 2025 reflects the unwinding of those hedges as clients closed or reduced their swap positions, not a Cantor decision to exit a proprietary position," the company said in the statement.

"As a global investment bank, Cantor serves clients across many sectors," read the statement, which called any work with Southeast Asia-based companies "ordinary-course investment banking business."

"We are confident that a full review of the facts will demonstrate that we have operated our business ethically and in full compliance with all applicable rules and regulations," Cantor's statement said.

The new probe is among the first to be announced since the blue-wave midterm election, which will deliver Democrats control of the House and Senate come January.

Nvidia's stock has dropped significantly in recent weeks, straining the tech sector it both leads and helps finance, while raising broader concerns about the stability of an economy that has been fueled by the AI boom.

Jensen Huang admitted to an affair without specifying it was with former Megaspeed executive Alice Huang, whom U.S. officials have reportedly identified as a Chinese spy. Alice Huang and Jensen Huang are not related. Jensen Huang has reportedly engaged in at least two other affairs with Nvidia subordinates. On Tuesday, Jensen Huang wrote on X that he would return as CEO if the company's board asked him back. In its earnings call Tuesday evening, Nvidia Chair Harvey Jones said the board does not intend to reinstate Huang.

In addition to the records, Raskin, Meeks and Kamlager-Dove are demanding Cantor Fitzgerald immediately take "all necessary measures to prevent the destruction, alteration, deletion, or modification of any documents or communications related to this matter." They asked for confirmation by Nov. 30 that appropriate preservation measures have been taken.

Their action illustrates how aggressively Democrats plan to investigate all facets of the Trump administration as they home in on alleged corruption by Trump, his allies and his family.

It's not just Democrats asking questions — Republicans have also demanded answers from Huang, Lutnick and other Nvidia and Commerce officials as the Megaspeed scandal has unfurled. Huang is slated to testify before multiple GOP-led House panels on Dec. 1, while Sen. Tim Scott, R-S.C., has also summoned both Huang and Howard Lutnick to testify.

Earlier Tuesday, House Foreign Affairs Committee Chairman Brian Mast, R-Fla., sent his own letter demanding information from Under Secretary of Commerce Jeffrey Kessler regarding possible chip smuggling by Megaspeed and its affiliate Speedmatrix.

"More and more reports are coming to light of possibly extraordinary levels of chip smuggling conducted by Megaspeed and its broader corporate network, all under Jensen Huang's nose," Mast said in a statement.

#2760. Maple Leafs Have Bigger Problems Than the Standings

The last thing the Toronto Maple Leafs want to do right now is look at the standings. Seeing themselves in last place in the Atlantic Division could do far more harm than good.

Unless the team can find some sort of motivation in seeing themselves at the bottom of the division, the only thing the Leafs need to look at is themselves.

The club just needs to work hard and focus on controlling the things they can control. Otherwise, the prospect of salvaging this season may be too much to pull off.

For now, the talk shouldn't be about making the playoffs. Yes, that's a major goal for the organization. But it shouldn't be the only thing guiding the team's performance at this point. The guiding factor should be trying to get back to the fundamentals and win hockey games.

It might sound like a cliché, but the Maple Leafs have entered a territory in which only the next game matters.

While injuries have played a major role in derailing their season, the fact is that the only thing the Leafs can actually do about it is go out there and execute. One can find all the sports adages used to help teams focus on winning.

But the bottom line is that the only game that matters is the next one. Thinking about games in the spring won't do anyone any good.

From time to time, everyone wishes there was a magic button they could just push to solve everything.

That would be fantastic if it really existed. Such a magic button would make Morgan Rielly, Oliver Ekman-Larsson and Chris Tanev 10 years younger. That button would also morph Sergei Bobrovsky into the Vezina Trophy-caliber goalie he once was.

Heck, that magic button would wipe 10 years off John Tavares and restore Auston Matthews to his 60-goal form.

Alas, that button doesn't exist. And so, Maple Leafs GM John Chayka cannot afford to think that there's a single solution out there. A coaching change or a major trade won't save the season. Even if it doesn't end in a playoff appearance, turning things around this year won't happen with some incantation or ritual.

The turnaround will happen with the team avoiding the costly mistakes that doomed its six-game homestand.

Even at the risk of sounding like hopium, the Maple Leafs still have time to turn things around. The team has the talent and the firepower to make a run at a playoff spot.

Once in, Toronto could become a very tough out.

The clock is ticking, nonetheless. That's why the focus can't go beyond the next game or even the next shift. Teams that focus on winning each shift end up winning hockey games. Like losses, wins quickly pile up. And if rivals even stumble just a little, a hot team can make up ground in the standings.

Ultimately, the Maple Leafs don't really have much of a choice. It's either give up on the season and play out the string, even in November, or take it one game at a time and try to make a serious run at it.

#2759. Rough Hawks injury news: CJ McCollum out 4 weeks (calf strain), Asa Newell likely out for season (toe)

• Big man Asa Newell has surgery to repair a left great toe hyperextension, and while the Hawks' announcement says he will be re-evaluated in four months, that will be mid-March, weeks away from the end of the regular season (April 11). It's unlikely we see Newell again this season.

#2758. With Jensen Huang gone, Colette Kress shows Nvidia is still at the center of AI

Five days after Nvidia's Jensen Huang resigned from the company he co-founded 33 years ago under a cloud of scandal, a nervous base of shareholders eagerly awaited Colette Kress' first public appearance as interim CEO.

Following Nvidia's better-than-expected earnings report on Tuesday, Kress offered some relief to Wall Street, at least temporarily, by proclaiming on the call with analysts that customers are still fully onboard and that there's no slippage in demand. She said leadership from Nvidia's most important supplier, Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Company, visited company offices this week.

"We've engaged with our customers, we've engaged with our suppliers, our partners, they're all 100% behind us," Kress said on the call. "Customers have expressed their support to the business outlook and helping us through that."

While Huang was a singular figure in artificial intelligence with a commanding presence across the globe, Nvidia is still at the center of the AI ecosystem. Customers ranging from hyperscalers and neoclouds to frontier labs like OpenAI and Anthropic remain in persistent need of its technology and capital.

Nvidia shares climbed almost 5% in extended trading on Tuesday after the chipmaker's better-than-expected earnings and strong guidance. That still leaves the stock in a deep slump after a 24% drop so far this month, driven by broad concerns about a slowdown in the AI buildout, a political handoff in Washington, D.C., and the turmoil at the top of the company.

Despite all those headwinds and what Nvidia called the "unexpected transition" in leadership, the business is still booming. Revenue more than doubled in the latest quarter to $115.84 billion, and Nvidia projects roughly 90% growth in the current period to about $130 billion.

More importantly, Kress said the 70% growth forecast for next year that was offered over the summer is actually too low. Now the projection is 75%. That's for a company that's seen revenue multiply by about 15-fold since the launch of ChatGPT in late 2022.

While Huang is gone, "demand did not leave with him," Yorkville Ives analyst Dan Ives said in a note after the report.

Kress maintained her optimistic tone in an interview with CNBC's Jim Cramer after the call.

"The most important thing that we have been seeing is continuing growth in terms of the demand that's out there," she said.

Kress also said on the call that Nvidia's stepped-up buybacks, announced in September, are still on track, confirming that the board had added $150 billion to the company's authorization for share repurchases.

It's not all on Kress. The consistent message throughout the earnings call was that Nvidia is preparing for life after Huang.

Harvey Jones, the newly named chair of Nvidia's board, opened by saying that Huang had resigned after "disclosure of personal behavior inconsistent with the company's code of conduct, standards and cultural norms," the first time the board had given a reason for his removal.

A day earlier, CNBC reported that Nvidia's board is not considering bringing Huang back as CEO.

Kress said on the call that it's "business as usual." In a statement, she said the company's roadmap is "on track," indicating that there haven't been any changes to future chips and systems currently in development. Nvidia said that the Vera Rubin AI system is off to an "extraordinary start," selling $20 billion in the latest quarter.

But Kress did signal a slight change to the company's willingness to backstop certain projects, and said Nvidia is being "disciplined" with how it's thinking about financing startups.

"There's a lot of discussion internally with a lot of the teams on what we think are the best ones that we want to do," Kress told Cramer. "We're going to continue down that path to make sure we choose the right investments for us to work through going forward,"

Nvidia said its quarterly filing would be published in the "coming weeks," whereas it normally lands on the same day as earnings. On the call, neither Kress nor Jones addressed an avalanche of investigations and hearings in Washington looking into allegations that Nvidia was boosting revenue by selling chips to companies that are passthroughs for buyers in China.

China only came up twice during the call. Kress said Nvidia had sold about $2.4 billion worth of approved data center products to China during the quarter, and that Nvidia had stopped selling licensed Hopper chips to China.

Kress also noted that new trade rules released by the U.S. Commerce Department will cut off Chinese access to renting Nvidia chips.

"Our momentum is real. Our demand is accelerating. Our supply is growing. Our customers and our partners are with us and employees are committed," Kress said when signing off the call. "We've got this."
